<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the ordinary-double-point gap conjecture for boundary-zero complex algebraic klt germs in every dimension: a singular <i>n</i>-dimensional germ has normalized volume at most $`2(n-1)^n`$, with equality precisely at an analytic ordinary double point.

- **claim-ordinary-double-point-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $n\ge2$, and let $x$ be a singular closed point of a normal complex algebraic $n$-fold $X$. Suppose that $K_X$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and that $X$ is klt near $x$, with zero boundary. Then $$\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)\le2(n-1)^n.$$ Equality holds if and only if $$(X,x)_{\mathrm{an}}\cong \left(\{z_0^2+\cdots+z_n^2=0\}\subset\mathbb C^{n+1},0\right).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds normalized volume for every singular complex algebraic klt germ in the stated boundary-zero setting and characterizes equality by the analytic ordinary double point.
- **claim-global-fano-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-global)): Let $V$ be an $n$-dimensional K-semistable $\mathbb Q$-Fano variety, where $n\ge2$. If $V$ is singular, then $$(-K_V)^n\le 2\left(\frac{n^2-1}{n}\right)^n<2n^n.$$ If $V\not\cong\mathbb P^n$, then $(-K_V)^n\le2n^n$, with equality precisely when $V$ is a smooth quadric $Q^n\subset\mathbb P^{n+1}$ or $\mathbb P^1\times\mathbb P^{n-1}$. These two descriptions coincide for $n=2$.
  - Explanation: Gives a strict improvement of the anticanonical volume bound for singular K-semistable Fano varieties. The larger bound has the stated smooth equality classification; no equality classification is asserted for the smaller singular bound.
- **claim-smooth-factor-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-cone-product)): Let $y\in Y$ be an $N$-dimensional klt germ, where $N\ge1$. Then $$d((y,0),Y\times\mathbb A^1)\le d(y,Y).$$
  - Explanation: Adding a smooth affine-line factor cannot increase normalized volume density. The proof supplies a Gauss extension of each divisorial test rather than asserting a product equality.
- **claim-associated-graded-embedding-dimension** (bound, [source](#stmt-cone-edim)): Let $v$ be a finite-discrepancy valuation of a klt germ, with finitely generated positively graded associated graded ring $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_v R$. Then $$\mathop{\mathrm{edim}}(R)\le\mathop{\mathrm{edim}}(\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_v R).$$
  - Explanation: Homogeneous generators of the associated graded ring lift to generators of the cotangent space of the original germ, giving the embedding-dimension comparison.
- **claim-ordinary-double-point-minimum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-end-odp-value)): For $n\ge2$, the ordinary double point $Q_n$ has $$\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(0,Q_n)=2(n-1)^n.$$
  - Explanation: Computes the infimum over all centered valuations at an ordinary double point. Uniqueness and orthogonal symmetry identify a minimizing valuation with a rescaling of ordinary degree.
- **claim-fourfold-base-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inp-fourfold)): Let $x$ be a singular closed point of a normal complex algebraic fourfold $X$. Suppose that $K_X$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and $X$ is klt near $x$, with zero boundary. Then $$\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)\le162.$$ Equality holds if and only if the complex analytic germ is $$\left(\{z_0^2+z_1^2+z_2^2+z_3^2+z_4^2=0\},0\right).$$ No isolatedness, complete-intersection, quotient, or smoothability assumption is made.
  - Explanation: Restates the unrestricted fourfold companion input, including its analytic equality characterization. This supplies the remaining base case for the induction.
- **claim-density-preserving-cone-reduction** (construction, [source](#stmt-cone-reduction)): Under <a href="#cone:eq-induction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cone:eq-induction">[cone:eq-induction]</a>, there is a singular K-polystable Fano cone $C=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}S$, with vertex $o$, such that $$d(o,C)=d(x,X),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{edim}}(X,x)\le\mathop{\mathrm{edim}}(C,o).$$ The puncture $C\setminus\{o\}$ is smooth, $S$ is a normal Gorenstein ring, and its canonical module has a homogeneous generator. The cone has zero boundary.
  - Explanation: Stable degeneration produces a singular K-polystable cone with the original density and an upper bound for the original embedding dimension. Smoothness of the puncture and Gorenstein structure are derived conclusions.
- **claim-fast-primitive-gradings** (construction, [source](#stmt-cone-approximation)): There are primitive positive integral cocharacters $\zeta_i$, of canonical weights $r_i=A(\zeta_i)\in\mathbb Z_{>0}$, such that $$\label{cone:eq-fast} \zeta_i=\frac{r_i}{n}\xi+\epsilon_i,\qquad \epsilon_i\longrightarrow0$$ in the real cocharacter space. If the ray of $\xi$ is rational, the sequence may be chosen constant and exact. Otherwise $r_i\to\infty$.* *The normalized volumes of the degree valuations $\operatorname{wt}_{\zeta_i}$ tend to $\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(o,C)$. For any fixed finite set of homogeneous algebra generators, their $\zeta_i$-degrees lie between $cr_i$ and $Cr_i$ for positive constants $c,C$. Consequently the largest stabilizer order $m_{\max,i}$ on the puncture is $O(r_i)$.
  - Explanation: Approximates the minimizing Reeb ray with absolute cocharacter error tending to zero. Volume convergence and generator-degree bounds hold on each fixed cone; the rational case admits an exact constant sequence.
- **claim-faithful-quotient-orbifold** (construction, [source](#stmt-cone-orbifold)): For a primitive positive integral grading $\zeta$, of canonical weight $r$, let $$B=[(C\setminus\{o\})/\mathbb G_m].$$ Then $B$ is a smooth effective proper orbifold of dimension $N$, with projective coarse space $\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}S$. It has a faithful ample weight-one line bundle $L$ such that $$\label{cone:eq-section-ring} S_j=H^0(B,jL),\qquad -K_B=rL.$$ Every stabilizer is a finite cyclic group. At a point of order $m$, a smooth transverse uniformizer carries a faithful $\mu_m$-action, and $L$ has a faithful fiber character.
  - Explanation: Retains the smooth quotient stack and its faithful weight-one line bundle. The section-ring and canonical-weight identities preserve the stabilizer characters needed later.
- **claim-small-stabilizer-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-small-classification)): Let $\zeta_i$ be primitive positive integral gradings satisfying $$\zeta_i=\frac{r_i}{n}\xi+\epsilon_i, \qquad r_i=A(\zeta_i),\qquad \\|\epsilon_i\\|\longrightarrow0, \qquad m_{\max}(\zeta_i)\leq\frac{r_i}{N}. \label{small:fast-sequence}$$ For all sufficiently large $i$, every stabilizer is trivial and $r_i\in\{N,N+1\}$. More precisely, the polarized quotient is $$(B_i,L_i)\cong \begin{cases} (\mathbb P^N,\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^N}(1)),&r_i=N+1,\\ (Q^N,\mathcal O_{Q^N}(1)),&r_i=N, \end{cases}$$ where $Q^N\subset\mathbb P^{N+1}$ is a smooth quadric. Consequently the cone $C$ is respectively smooth or an ordinary double point. The assertion includes the constant exact primitive grading when the ray of $\xi$ is rational.
  - Explanation: Fast gradings in the small-stabilizer range eventually have free action. Their polarized quotients are projective space or a smooth quadric, so the affine cone is smooth or an ordinary double point.
- **claim-fixed-lift-deformation-dimension** (bound, [source](#stmt-small-deformation)): Let $b_1',\ldots,b_n'\in\{1,\ldots,m\}$ represent the characters of $\mu_m$ on $T_{C,P}$, with the trivial character represented by $m$. Every component through $F$ of the scheme of homogeneous maps $F':\mathbb A^2(1,m)\to C$ satisfying $F'(0,1)=P$ has dimension at least $$\chi=n+\frac rm-\sum_{j=1}^n\frac{b_j'}m. \label{small:chi}$$ In particular this dimension is at least $n$, except possibly when $r/m=N$ and the entire tangent action at $P$ is trivial.
  - Explanation: Orbifold Riemann–Roch bounds the dimension of each component of homogeneous maps with fixed lift. The exceptional case requires both the endpoint canonical-weight ratio and a trivial tangent action.
- **claim-finite-surjective-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-small-finite-evaluation)): The morphism in Equation <a href="#small:evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="small:evaluation">[small:evaluation]</a> is finite and surjective. Its fiber over $o$ is supported at the unique contracted map.
  - Explanation: The evaluation map from the chosen deformation component is finite and surjective. Its vertex fiber is supported at the contracted map, as shown using minimal curve degree and proper limiting maps.
- **claim-negative-weight-symmetric-tensor** (construction, [source](#stmt-small-norm-tensor)): Let $b$ be the degree of the finite evaluation in Lemma <a href="#small:finite-evaluation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="small:finite-evaluation">12</a>. There is a nonzero algebraic section $$s\in H^0(C^\circ,\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^bT_{C^\circ})$$ of integral-grading weight $-bm$.
  - Explanation: The symmetric product of translation vectors over the generic sheets of finite evaluation descends to a nonzero regular tensor. Taking a product avoids the cancellation that could occur with a trace.
- **claim-reeb-tensor-weight-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-small-tensor-bound)): Let $b\geq1$, and let $s$ be a nonzero homogeneous algebraic section of $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^bT_{C^\circ}$, of $\xi$-weight $\tau$, with vector fields weighted as derivations. Then $$\tau\geq-b.$$
  - Explanation: Bochner subharmonicity on the Ricci-flat cone prevents a nonzero homogeneous symmetric tangent tensor from having radial norm degree below the stated threshold.
- **claim-rank-uniform-character-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-small-character-estimate)): There is a constant $c>0$ with the following property for all sufficiently close gradings in Equation <a href="#small:fast-sequence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="small:fast-sequence">[small:fast-sequence]</a>. If a nonzero rank-$b$ tensor on $C^\circ$ has integral-grading weight $-bm$, with $m>0$, then it has a nonzero torus eigencomponent of character $\chi$ satisfying $$\\|\chi\\|\leq cb.$$ Consequently, if the tensor is as in Lemma <a href="#small:norm-tensor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="small:norm-tensor">13</a>, then $$m\leq\frac rn+c\\|\epsilon\\|. \label{small:m-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Pairing tensors with differentials of fixed algebra generators gives character control uniform in tensor rank. Combining that control with the Reeb weight bound bounds the endpoint stabilizer order.
- **claim-jet-semigroup-and-cone-setup** (construction, [source](#stmt-jet-setup)): The following assertions hold.* 1. *$\Gamma$ is an additive semigroup with one-dimensional leaves, and its generated group is exactly $\Lambda$. Every finite compatible Taylor jet is realized by sections of all sufficiently large degrees in each admissible progression.* 2. *$K$ is a full-dimensional closed convex cone, contains the positive constant ray, projects onto the whole exponent orthant, and satisfies $K=\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{int}}K}$. Its bounded-degree sections are bounded. More precisely, uniformly in the chosen point and nearby grading, $$\label{jet:bezout-bound} \|\gamma\|:=\sum_i\gamma_i\le C_C\frac jr \qquad ((j,\gamma)\in K).$$ Its scaled image has the form $$\label{jet:epigraph} \widetilde K=\{(D_0,x):x\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^N,\ D_0\ge f(x)\},$$ where $f$ is finite, positively homogeneous, convex, and $$\label{jet:f-coercive} f(x)\ge c_C\|x\|\qquad(x\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^N)$$ for a constant $c_C>0$ independent of the nearby grading and chosen point.* 3. *In the scaled coordinates the lattice density is $s$. If $\ell$ is a linear functional strictly positive on $\widetilde K\setminus\{0\}$, then $$\label{jet:count} \begin{aligned} &\#\{\gamma'\in\widetilde\Gamma:\ell(\gamma')<q\}\\ &\qquad=s\,\operatorname{Leb}\{(D_0,x)\in\widetilde K:\ell(D_0,x)<1\}\,q^n+o(q^n), \end{aligned}$$ with Lebesgue measure in the scaled coordinates. No saturation assumption on $\Gamma$ is required.* 4. *The vector $$\label{jet:canonical-vector} c_0=(r,\mathbf1)\qquad\text{in the original }(j,\gamma) \text{ coordinates}$$ belongs to $\Lambda$. Its scaled coordinates are $(N,\mathbf1)$.
  - Explanation: Establishes the leading-term semigroup, its full congruence lattice, bounded convex cone, scaled epigraph, and canonical lattice point. Its asymptotic count does not require exact saturation.
- **claim-degree-taylor-test-valuations** (construction, [source](#stmt-jet-test)): For $\delta\in\mathbb Q_{\ge0}$, the rule $$\label{jet:test-rule} v_\delta\Bigl(\sum_j s_j\Bigr) =\min_{s_j\ne0}\left\{\frac{Nj}{r} +\delta\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_z(s_j)\right\}$$ defines a valuation centered at the vertex. It is divisorial up to positive rescaling, and $$\begin{aligned} A_C(v_\delta)&=N+N\delta,\label{jet:test-discrepancy}\\ A_C(v_\delta)^n\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(v_\delta) &=N^n(1+\delta)^n s \int_{\mathbb R_{\ge0}^N}e^{-f(x)-\delta\|x\|}\,dx. \label{jet:test-volume} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Combines original degree and transverse Taylor order into centered test valuations, with discrepancy and volume determined by the scaled jet cone.
- **claim-near-minimal-moment-inequalities** (bound, [source](#stmt-jet-moments)): Along the fixed-cone sequence of gradings, there are numbers $\eta_\nu\ge0$ tending to zero such that $$\label{jet:moment-inequalities} H\ge\frac2s,\qquad \mathbb E[(1+B_0(X))e^{-B_0(X)}]\ge-\eta_\nu H.$$ For an exact minimizing rational grading one may take $\eta_\nu=0$.
  - Explanation: The density hypothesis bounds the zeroth moment from below. Near-minimality controls the right derivative of the test-volume function and gives the second moment inequality with an error vanishing along the fixed-cone sequence.
- **claim-universal-exponential-supremum** (bound, [source](#stmt-prob-bound)): For $u=4/3$ one has $$\label{prob:universal-bound} \sup_{N\ge1}\ \sup_{\substack{a\in\mathbb R^N\\\sum_i a_i=0}} \frac{e^u}{u}\mathbb E g(u+a\cdot X) =C_*:=\frac34e^{4/3}-\frac e3<2.$$ The supremum over dimensions is approached by the vectors $(2,-2/(N-1),\ldots,-2/(N-1))$ as $N\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the sharp dimension-uniform supremum for the truncated exponential expectation under the sum-zero constraint. The value is approached through increasing dimension rather than asserted to be attained in a finite dimension.
- **claim-exponential-logconcavity-and-centroid-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prob-logconcavity)): A nonzero mean-zero linear combination of independent mean-one exponentials has a positive continuous log-concave density on the whole real line. Adding further independent scaled exponentials, with either sign of coefficient, preserves these properties. If $Z$ has such a density, then $$\label{prob:grunbaum} \frac1e\le\mathbb P(Z\le\mathbb EZ)\le1-\frac1e.$$
  - Explanation: Mixed-sign exponential sums with zero mean have positive continuous log-concave densities on the whole real line. Convolution preserves these properties and yields the stated barycenter half-space bounds.
- **claim-strict-exponential-single-crossing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prob-single-crossing)): Let $Z$ have a positive log-concave probability density on $\mathbb R$, and let $E$ be an independent mean-one exponential. If $$\mathbb E k(Z+c_*E)=0,$$ then $\mathbb E k(Z+cE)>0$ for every $c<c_*$ and $\mathbb E k(Z+cE)<0$ for every $c>c_*$.
  - Explanation: A zero of the kernel expectation separates strictly positive and strictly negative values as the exponential coefficient increases. The proof treats both parameter signs and the transition through zero.
- **claim-finite-local-maximizer-shape** (classification, [source](#stmt-prob-critical-points)): Fix $u>0$ and $N\ge2$. Every nonzero finite local maximum of $$a\longmapsto\mathbb E g\left(u+\sum_{i=1}^N a_iX_i\right), \qquad \sum_i a_i=0,$$ has, after permutation, the form $$\label{prob:exceptional-vector} a=(t,-t/(N-1),\ldots,-t/(N-1))\qquad(t>0).$$
  - Explanation: Every nonzero finite local maximizer has a single positive exceptional coefficient and equal remaining coefficients. Stationarity and size bias exclude additional coefficient values; a splitting Hessian excludes repeated higher coefficients.
- **claim-canonical-jet-interiority** (theorem, [source](#stmt-jet-interior)): For every sufficiently large grading in the subsequence under consideration, $$\label{jet:interior-conclusion} c_0=(r,\mathbf1)\in\mathop{\mathrm{int}}K \qquad\text{in the original degree coordinates}.$$ Equivalently, $f(\mathbf1)<N$. For an exact minimizing rational grading with $m\ge r/N$, the conclusion holds without passage to a limit.
  - Explanation: The canonical lattice point lies strictly inside the original-degree jet cone for sufficiently far large-stabilizer gradings. An exact minimizing rational grading gives the conclusion directly.
- **claim-orbifold-adjoint-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-adj-vanishing)): Let $\mathcal X$ be a smooth proper Deligne–Mumford stack over $\mathbb C$, with trivial generic stabilizer and projective coarse space. Let $\Theta$ be an effective rational divisor with simple normal crossing support and coefficients less than one. If $D$ is a Cartier divisor and $D-(K_{\mathcal X}+\Theta)$ is nef and big, then $$H^q(\mathcal X,\mathcal O_{\mathcal X}(D))=0\qquad(q>0).$$
  - Explanation: Proves adjoint cohomology vanishing on the stated smooth proper stack by exact coarse pushdown and a klt coarse boundary that retains divisorial stabilizer corrections.
- **claim-canonically-shifted-adjoint-lifting** (construction, [source](#stmt-adj-implication)): For the orbifold jet data above, $$\label{adj:adjoint-implication} (j,\alpha)\in\Lambda,\qquad (j,\alpha)+c_0\in\mathop{\mathrm{int}}K \quad\Longrightarrow\quad (j,\alpha)\in\Gamma.$$
  - Explanation: A compatible lattice index whose canonical translate is interior is realized as an actual Taylor initial. Weighted blowup, multiplier-ideal membership, and adjoint vanishing supply the lift without assuming canonical interiority beforehand.
- **claim-rational-polyhedral-saturated-jet-semigroup** (classification, [source](#stmt-adj-saturation)): Suppose $c_0\in\mathop{\mathrm{int}}K$. Then the following statements hold:* 1. *$K$ is a rational polyhedral cone.* 2. *Every facet has a primitive nonnegative support form $\eta\in\Lambda^*$ satisfying $\eta(c_0)=1$.* 3. *The semigroup and its interior lattice points satisfy $$\label{adj:saturation-identities} \Gamma=K\cap\Lambda, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{int}}K\cap\Lambda=c_0+\Gamma.$$* *In particular, $\Gamma$ is a finitely generated saturated affine semigroup with group $\Lambda$. These conclusions apply to every sufficiently far grading and chosen point covered by Theorem <a href="#jet:interior" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="jet:interior">23</a>, including its exact rational case.
  - Explanation: Canonical interiority and adjoint lifting force rational polyhedrality, primitive facet height, exact saturation, and the canonical translation description of interior lattice points.
- **claim-taylor-graded-ring-and-compatible-basis** (construction, [source](#stmt-filt-first-ring)): The algebra $\overline S$ is finitely generated, normal, Cohen–Macaulay, and Gorenstein. Its canonical module has a homogeneous generator of bidegree $(r,N)$. There is a section $v\in S_m$ whose initial $\overline v$ is $\tau^m$. In particular $T(v)=0$, and $$\label{filt:laurent-product} \overline S[\overline v^{-1}] \simeq \mathbb C[\overline v,\overline v^{-1},u_1,\ldots,u_N], \qquad T(u_i)=1,\quad T(\overline v)=0.$$ The ring $D=\overline S/(\overline v)$ is an equidimensional Cohen–Macaulay Gorenstein ring of dimension $N$, with $$\label{filt:D-series} \mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}_T D(z)=(1-z)^{-N}, \qquad \deg\omega_D=(r-m,N).$$ Its $T$-degree-zero piece is $\mathbb C$. A bihomogeneous vector-space basis of $D$ can be lifted to sections $e_i\in S$ such that, on putting $T_i=T(e_i)$, $$\label{filt:S-basis} \{v^a e_i:a\in\mathbb N\}$$ is a vector-space basis of $S$, compatible with original degree and $T$-order. The unique vector of this basis with $a+T_i=0$ is $1$.
  - Explanation: Transfers normal Gorenstein semigroup structure to the Taylor graded ring. A constant-initial section gives a Laurent polynomial localization, the quotient Hilbert series, and a compatible basis of the original ring.
- **claim-rees-canonical-form-and-discrepancy** (bound, [source](#stmt-filt-discrepancy)): Suppose $F$ respects original degree, has finite length in each original homogeneous piece, and $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_F S$ is finitely generated. Suppose also that $\mathfrak X=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R_F$ is normal and Gorenstein, and that $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_F S$ is Cohen–Macaulay and Gorenstein with a canonical generator of bidegree $(r,Q)$. Then $R_F$ is finite type and its absolute canonical module has a homogeneous generator of bidegree $(r,Q-1)$, which on $\lambda\ne0$ is $$\label{filt:canonical-form} \Omega_{\mathfrak X} =c\lambda^{-Q}\Omega_C\wedge d\lambda, \qquad c\in\mathbb C^*.$$ For a quasi-monomial valuation $V$ centered on $\mathfrak X$ over $\lambda=0$, put $\ell=V(\lambda)>0$ and $w=V\|_{\mathbb C(C)}$. If $w$ is centered at $o$, then $$\label{filt:discrepancy-inequality} A_C(w)\leq A_{\mathfrak X}(V)+(Q-1)\ell.$$ The same inequality holds whenever the discrepancies involved are finite and defined by valuation retractions.
  - Explanation: Tracks the canonical generator through the extended Rees family and compares a cone valuation's discrepancy with that of its extension. The filtration shift and parameter contribution must both be retained.
- **claim-reduced-constant-section-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-filt-reduced)): Under Equation <a href="#filt:volume-hypothesis" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="filt:volume-hypothesis">[filt:volume-hypothesis]</a>, $D$ is reduced.
  - Explanation: The volume lower bound rules out repeated components of the constant-section divisor. Generic reducedness and Cohen–Macaulayness then make its quotient reduced.
- **claim-multiplicative-second-filtration** (construction, [source](#stmt-filt-structure)): The filtration $W$ is multiplicative, with $$\label{filt:second-graded} G=\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_WS\simeq D[\mathtt v],\qquad \deg_W\mathtt v=1,\qquad \deg_W\|_D=T.$$ Here $\mathtt v$ is a regular polynomial variable of original degree $m$. Moreover $$\label{filt:G-series} \mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}_WG(z)=(1-z)^{-n},\qquad \deg\omega_G=(r,n).$$ The $W$-Rees space is a finite-type normal Gorenstein variety. Its canonical generator has bidegree $(r,n-1)$ and, on $\lambda\ne0$, is $c\lambda^{-n}\Omega_C\wedge d\lambda$.
  - Explanation: The second filtration assigns positive cost to the constant Taylor direction and has associated graded algebra equal to a polynomial extension of the reduced quotient. Its Rees total space is normal and Gorenstein even without normality of the central ring.
- **claim-free-grading-polynomial-rigidity** (classification, [source](#stmt-filt-free-grading)): Let $E$ be a finitely generated positively graded reduced equidimensional Cohen–Macaulay $\mathbb C$-algebra of dimension $N\geq2$, with $E_0=\mathbb C$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}E(z)=(1-z)^{-N}$. If the grading action has trivial stabilizer at every nonvertex closed point of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}E$, then $E$ is a polynomial ring on $N$ generators of degree one.
  - Explanation: Under the specified ring properties and Hilbert series, a free grading action on the puncture makes weight one an actual ample line bundle and forces a polynomial ring generated in degree one.
- **claim-nontrivial-second-stabilizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-filt-stabilizer)): There is a nonvertex closed point of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}D$ whose $T$-grading stabilizer has order $h\geq2$.
  - Explanation: Singularity of the original cone prevents the quotient from having a free grading everywhere on its puncture. A nonvertex point therefore has the stated nontrivial second stabilizer.
- **claim-uniformizing-slice-and-valuation-tests** (construction, [source](#stmt-boot-slice-tests)): There is a normal Gorenstein algebraic germ $y\in Y$ of dimension $n$, preserved by $\mu_h$, with a nonzero function $\lambda_Y\in\mathfrak m_{Y,y}$ of character $-1$, such that $Y$ is klt away from $\lambda_Y=0$. Every quasi-monomial valuation $u$ centered at $y$ gives a quasi-monomial valuation $w$ centered at $o\in C$. Writing $\ell=u(\lambda_Y)>0$, one has $$\label{boot:basic-test} A_C(w)\leq N\ell+A_Y(u), \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)\leq \ell^{-n}.$$ The comparison is valid before klt at $y$ has been established.
  - Explanation: Constructs a same-dimensional normal Gorenstein slice preserved by the second stabilizer. Restricting quasi-monomial valuations gives centered cone valuations with discrepancy and volume bounds before klt at the slice point has been proved.
- **claim-slice-klt** (theorem, [source](#stmt-boot-slice-klt)): The germ $y\in Y$ is klt.
  - Explanation: A hypothetical non-klt divisor on the slice gives centered tests contradicting the cone's volume lower bound, so the constructed slice is klt.
- **claim-slice-singularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-boot-slice-singular)): The germ $y\in Y$ is singular.
  - Explanation: Smoothness of the slice would produce a nonvertex fixed formal section in an equivariant deformation whose generic fiber has only the origin fixed. This contradiction proves that the slice remains singular.
- **claim-equal-leading-character-colengths** (bound, [source](#stmt-boot-character-shares)): Let $R=\mathcal O_{Y,y}$ and let $\mathfrak a_t=\{g\in R:u_*(g)\geq t\}$. For every character $\chi\in\mathbb Z/h\mathbb Z$, $$\dim_\mathbb C(R/\mathfrak a_t)_\chi =\frac{\sigma}{h\,n!}t^n+o(t^n) \qquad(t\longrightarrow\infty).$$
  - Explanation: The invariant minimizing valuation has asymptotically equal colength in every cyclic character. Multiplication by the nonzero parameter of primitive character compares the cutoffs without requiring a tangent-representation assumption.
- **claim-refined-character-slice-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-boot-refined-estimate)): For the minimizing slice valuation and its restriction, $$\label{boot:refined-volume} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)\leq \min_{0\leq x\leq1} \left\{\frac{x^n}{\ell^n} +(1-x)^n\frac{\sigma}{h}\right\}.$$
  - Explanation: Splits cone colength into a filtration cutoff and a single character quotient on the slice. Optimizing the cutoff gives the refined upper bound for the restricted valuation volume.
- **claim-holder-supremum-bootstrap** (bound, [source](#stmt-boot-holder)): For the fixed cone and large-stabilizer construction, $$\begin{aligned} d(o,C) &\leq \frac{\bigl((N/n)\ell+A'/n\bigr)^n} {\bigl(\ell^{n/N}+(h/\sigma)^{1/N}\bigr)^N} \label{boot:optimized}\\ &\leq (N/n)^n+(A'/n)^n\sigma/h \leq \frac{p_n}{2}+\frac{M_n}{2}. \label{boot:bootstrap-inequality} \end{aligned}$$ Equality in the middle, Hölder, inequality holds precisely when $$\label{boot:holder-equality} (A')^N\ell\,\frac{\sigma}{h}=N^N.$$
  - Explanation: Explicit minimization and Hölder's inequality bound cone density by the average of the ordinary-double-point threshold and the singular-density supremum. The slice enters through the definition of that supremum, not a same-dimensional theorem.
- **claim-inductive-singular-density-upper-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-boot-upper)): Assume the local ODP volume theorem in all dimensions smaller than $n$. Then $M_n\leq p_n$. Equivalently, every singular boundary-zero klt $n$-dimensional germ satisfies $$\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)\leq 2(n-1)^n.$$
  - Explanation: Applies the bootstrap to singular densities approaching their supremum and closes the upper-bound induction. Neither attainment of the supremum nor approximation estimates uniform across different cones are required.
- **claim-equality-valuation-identities** (classification, [source](#stmt-boot-equality-data)): One has $h=2$, $d(y,Y)=p_n$, and $A'=N\ell$. After rescaling $u_*$ so that $\ell=1$, the resulting restriction $w$ satisfies $$\label{boot:equality-identities} A'=N, \qquad \sigma=2, \qquad A_C(w)=2N, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)=2^{-N}, \qquad w=\frac{2N}{n}\operatorname{wt}_\xi.$$ The optimal splitting fraction in Equation <a href="#boot:refined-volume" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:refined-volume">[boot:refined-volume]</a> is $x=1/2$, and all inequalities producing that estimate are equalities at the level of leading coefficients.
  - Explanation: Equality in the bootstrap forces the second stabilizer order, slice density, discrepancy ratio, optimal split, and restriction valuation. Uniqueness identifies the restriction with a rescaling of the minimizing Reeb valuation.
- **claim-distinguished-section-strict-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-boot-strict-count)): In the normalization of Lemma <a href="#boot:equality-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:equality-data">40</a>, the section $v\in S_m$ used to define the two filtrations satisfies $w(v)\leq2$.
  - Explanation: A value above the stated threshold for the distinguished section would remove a positive leading-order portion of the first colength summand, contradicting the equality volume.
- **claim-integral-maximal-stabilizer-rounding** (bound, [source](#stmt-boot-rounding)): Along the fast gradings of the fixed equality cone, the large-stabilizer construction implies $Nm_{\max}\leq r$ for every sufficiently far term.
  - Explanation: Fixed-generator character control makes the changing distinguished section's Reeb-weight error absolutely small. The strict-count bound and integrality then return the maximal stabilizer to the small-stabilizer range.
- **claim-equality-cone-is-ordinary-double-point** (classification, [source](#stmt-boot-equality-cone)): If a singular cone obtained in Proposition <a href="#cone:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cone:reduction">7</a> satisfies $d(o,C)=p_n$, then it is the standard ordinary double point cone.
  - Explanation: Eventually every fast grading of an equality cone satisfies the small-stabilizer hypothesis. The small-stabilizer classification and singularity of the vertex leave only the standard ordinary double point cone.
- **claim-positive-weight-hypersurface-test** (bound, [source](#stmt-end-hypersurface-test)): Let $x\in X$ be a klt hypersurface germ of dimension $n$, with equation $F$ in regular ambient parameters $z_0,\ldots,z_n$. For positive rational weights $w_0,\ldots,w_n$, write $d_F$ for the weighted order of $F$. Then $$\label{end:eq-hypersurface-test} \widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)\le \frac{d_F\bigl(\sum_{i=0}^n w_i-d_F\bigr)^n} {\prod_{i=0}^n w_i}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the normalized-volume infimum using weighted primary ideals, inversion of adjunction, and multiplicity. The argument does not require the weighted initial equation to be irreducible or reduced.
- **claim-hypersurface-equality-rigidity** (classification, [source](#stmt-end-hypersurface)): If $x\in X$ is a singular klt hypersurface germ of dimension $n\ge2$, then $$\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)\le2(n-1)^n.$$ If equality holds, the quadratic part of a local equation is nondegenerate, and $(X,x)$ is analytically an ordinary double point.
  - Explanation: Higher multiplicity gives a strict bound, and perturbing a weight in a missing quadratic direction gives strictness for a degenerate quadratic part. Equality therefore forces an analytic ordinary double point.

```json
{
  "claims": [
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge2$, and let $x$ be a singular closed point of a normal complex algebraic $n$-fold $X$. Suppose that $K_X$ is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier and that $X$ is klt near $x$, with zero boundary.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-global",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be an $n$-dimensional K-semistable $\\mathbb Q$-Fano variety, where $n\\ge2$.         "source_id": "PM-037.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cone-approximation",
        "exact_quote": "There are primitive positive integral cocharacters $\\zeta_i$, of canonical weights $r_i=A(\\zeta_i)\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0}$, such that $$\\label{cone:eq-fast}\n             \\zeta_i=\\frac{r_i}{n}\\xi+\\epsilon_i,\\qquad\n             \\epsilon_i\\longrightarrow0$$ in the real cocharacter space. If the ray of $\\xi$ is rational, the sequence may be chosen constant and exact. Otherwise $r_i\\to\\infty$.*\n\n*The normalized volumes of the degree valuations $\\operatorname{wt}_{\\zeta_i}$ tend to $\\widehat{\\operatorname{vol}}(o,C)$. For any fixed finite set of homogeneous algebra generators, their $\\zeta_i$-degrees lie between $cr_i$ and $Cr_i$ for positive constants $c,C$. Consequently the largest stabilizer order $m_{\\max,i}$ on the puncture is $O(r_i)$.",

      "explanation": "Fast gradings in the small-stabilizer range eventually have free action. Their polarized quotients are projective space or a smooth quadric, so the affine cone is smooth or an ordinary double point.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-small-character-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $c>0$ with the following property for all sufficiently close gradings in Equation <a href=\"#small:fast-sequence\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"small:fast-sequence\">[small:fast-sequence]</a>.         "source_id": "PM-037.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-jet-setup",
        "exact_quote": "The following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *$\\Gamma$ is an additive semigroup with one-dimensional leaves, and its generated group is exactly $\\Lambda$. Every finite compatible Taylor jet is realized by sections of all sufficiently large degrees in each admissible progression.*\n\n\n\n2.  *$K$ is a full-dimensional closed convex cone, contains the positive constant ray, projects onto the whole exponent orthant, and satisfies $K=\\overline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{int}}K}$. Its bounded-degree sections are bounded. More precisely, uniformly in the chosen point and nearby grading, $$\\label{jet:bezout-bound}\n     |\\gamma|:=\\sum_i\\gamma_i\\le C_C\\frac jr\n                           \\qquad ((j,\\gamma)\\in K).$$ Its scaled image has the form $$\\label{jet:epigraph}\n     \\widetilde K=\\{(D_0,x):x\\in\\mathbb R_{\\ge0}^N,\\ D_0\\ge f(x)\\},$$ where $f$ is finite, positively homogeneous, convex, and $$\\label{jet:f-coercive}\n     f(x)\\ge c_C|x|\\qquad(x\\in\\mathbb R_{\\ge0}^N)$$ for a constant $c_C>0$ independent of the nearby grading and chosen point.*\n\n\n3.  *In the scaled coordinates the lattice density is $s$. If $\\ell$ is a linear functional strictly positive on $\\widetilde K\\setminus\\{0\\}$, then $$\\label{jet:count}\n     \\begin{aligned}\n     &\\#\\{\\gamma'\\in\\widetilde\\Gamma:\\ell(\\gamma')<q\\}\\\\\n     &\\qquad=s\\,\\operatorname{Leb}\\{(D_0,x)\\in\\widetilde K:\\ell(D_0,x)<1\\}\\,q^n+o(q^n),\n     \\end{aligned}$$ with Lebesgue measure in the scaled coordinates. No saturation assumption on $\\Gamma$ is required.*\n\n\n4.  *The vector $$\\label{jet:canonical-vector}\n     c_0=(r,\\mathbf1)\\qquad\\text{in the original }(j,\\gamma)\n     \\text{ coordinates}$$ belongs to $\\Lambda$. Its scaled coordinates are $(N,\\mathbf1)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-037.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "For $\\delta\\in\\mathbb Q_{\\ge0}$, the rule $$\\label{jet:test-rule}\n v_\\delta\\Bigl(\\sum_j s_j\\Bigr)\n   =\\min_{s_j\\ne0}\\left\\{\\frac{Nj}{r}\n                           +\\delta\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_z(s_j)\\right\\}$$ defines a valuation centered at the vertex.         "source_id": "PM-037.1"

      "exact_statement": "Along the fixed-cone sequence of gradings, there are numbers $\\eta_\\nu\\ge0$ tending to zero such that $$\\label{jet:moment-inequalities}\n H\\ge\\frac2s,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E[(1+B_0(X))e^{-B_0(X)}]\\ge-\\eta_\\nu H.$$ For an exact minimizing rational grading one may take $\\eta_\\nu=0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-filt-first-ring",
        "exact_quote": "The algebra $\\overline S$ is finitely generated, normal, Cohen–Macaulay, and Gorenstein. Its canonical module has a homogeneous generator of bidegree $(r,N)$. There is a section $v\\in S_m$ whose initial $\\overline v$ is $\\tau^m$. In particular $T(v)=0$, and $$\\label{filt:laurent-product}\n \\overline S[\\overline v^{-1}]\n \\simeq \\mathbb C[\\overline v,\\overline v^{-1},u_1,\\ldots,u_N],\n \\qquad T(u_i)=1,\\quad T(\\overline v)=0.$$ The ring $D=\\overline S/(\\overline v)$ is an equidimensional Cohen–Macaulay Gorenstein ring of dimension $N$, with $$\\label{filt:D-series}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hilb}}_T D(z)=(1-z)^{-N},\n \\qquad \\deg\\omega_D=(r-m,N).$$ Its $T$-degree-zero piece is $\\mathbb C$. A bihomogeneous vector-space basis of $D$ can be lifted to sections $e_i\\in S$ such that, on putting $T_i=T(e_i)$, $$\\label{filt:S-basis}\n \\{v^a e_i:a\\in\\mathbb N\\}$$ is a vector-space basis of $S$, compatible with original degree and $T$-order. The unique vector of this basis with $a+T_i=0$ is $1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-037.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-filt-first-ring",
          "exact_quote": "The algebra $\\overline S$ is finitely generated, normal, Cohen–Macaulay, and Gorenstein. Its canonical module has a homogeneous generator of bidegree $(r,N)$. There is a section $v\\in S_m$ whose initial $\\overline v$ is $\\tau^m$. In particular $T(v)=0$, and $$\\label{filt:laurent-product}\n \\overline S[\\overline v^{-1}]\n \\simeq \\mathbb C[\\overline v,\\overline v^{-1},u_1,\\ldots,u_N],\n \\qquad T(u_i)=1,\\quad T(\\overline v)=0.$$ The ring $D=\\overline S/(\\overline v)$ is an equidimensional Cohen–Macaulay Gorenstein ring of dimension $N$, with $$\\label{filt:D-series}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hilb}}_T D(z)=(1-z)^{-N},\n \\qquad \\deg\\omega_D=(r-m,N).$$ Its $T$-degree-zero piece is $\\mathbb C$. A bihomogeneous vector-space basis of $D$ can be lifted to sections $e_i\\in S$ such that, on putting $T_i=T(e_i)$, $$\\label{filt:S-basis}\n \\{v^a e_i:a\\in\\mathbb N\\}$$ is a vector-space basis of $S$, compatible with original degree and $T$-order. The unique vector of this basis with $a+T_i=0$ is $1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-037.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The algebra $\\overline S$ is finitely generated, normal, Cohen–Macaulay, and Gorenstein. Its canonical module has a homogeneous generator of bidegree $(r,N)$. There is a section $v\\in S_m$ whose initial $\\overline v$ is $\\tau^m$. In particular $T(v)=0$, and $$\\label{filt:laurent-product}\n \\overline S[\\overline v^{-1}]\n \\simeq \\mathbb C[\\overline v,\\overline v^{-1},u_1,\\ldots,u_N],\n \\qquad T(u_i)=1,\\quad T(\\overline v)=0.$$ The ring $D=\\overline S/(\\overline v)$ is an equidimensional Cohen–Macaulay Gorenstein ring of dimension $N$, with $$\\label{filt:D-series}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hilb}}_T D(z)=(1-z)^{-N},\n \\qquad \\deg\\omega_D=(r-m,N).$$ Its $T$-degree-zero piece is $\\mathbb C$. A bihomogeneous vector-space basis of $D$ can be lifted to sections $e_i\\in S$ such that, on putting $T_i=T(e_i)$, $$\\label{filt:S-basis}\n \\{v^a e_i:a\\in\\mathbb N\\}$$ is a vector-space basis of $S$, compatible with original degree and $T$-order. The unique vector of this basis with $a+T_i=0$ is $1$.",
      "explanation": "Transfers normal Gorenstein semigroup structure to the Taylor graded ring. A constant-initial section gives a Laurent polynomial localization, the quotient Hilbert series, and a compatible basis of the original ring.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "filt:semigroup-input",
          "exact_quote": "Fix one of the gradings and points furnished by Theorem <a href=\"#adj:saturation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"adj:saturation\">26</a>. We retain the original degree $j$ and put $n=N+1$, with $N\\geq4$. Thus $S=\\mathbb C[C]$ is a positively graded normal Gorenstein domain, its vertex $o$ is singular, and its canonical module has a homogeneous generator of original degree $r$. The Taylor semigroup and its lattice satisfy $$\\label{filt:semigroup-input}\n \\Gamma=K\\cap\\Lambda,\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{int}}(K)\\cap\\Lambda=c_0+\\Gamma,\\qquad\n c_0=(r,1,\\ldots,1).$$ Here $K$ is rational polyhedral. We also use the finite Taylor-order bound and the eventual realization of compatible jets from Proposition <a href=\"#jet:setup\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"jet:setup\">16</a>. The only volume hypothesis in this section is $$\\label{filt:volume-hypothesis}\n \\widehat{\\operatorname{vol}}(o,C)\\geq 2N^n.$$ The first filtration separates total Taylor order from original degree. We then assign positive cost to the constant Taylor direction as well. The resulting second graded ring has a nontrivial stabilizer, which will supply the same-dimensional slice in Section <a href=\"#boot:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"boot:section\">7</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-037.1"
        }
      ],
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Ordinary double point** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An ordinary double point is the analytic hypersurface germ of a nondegenerate quadratic form.
- **Divisorial log discrepancy** ([source](#pm-h-7)): If $E$ is a prime divisor on a smooth proper birational model $\mu\colon X'\to X$, its log discrepancy is $$A_X(E)=1+\operatorname{coeff}_E(K_{X'}-\mu^*K_X).$$
- **Valuation ideals and valuation volume** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a real valuation $v$ of $K(X)$, trivial on $\mathbb C$ and centered exactly at $x$, put $$\mathfrak a_t(v)=\{f\in R:v(f)\ge t\},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(v)=\lim_{t\to\infty} \frac{n!\,\mathop{\mathrm{length}}(R/\mathfrak a_t(v))}{t^n}.$$
- **Extended normalized volume of a valuation** ([source](#cone:eq-density)): Define $$\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}_X(v)= \begin{cases} A_X(v)^n\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(v),&A_X(v)<+\infty,\\ +\infty,&A_X(v)=+\infty. \end{cases}$$
- **Normalized volume and density of a germ** ([source](#cone:eq-density)): Our normalization is $$\label{cone:eq-density} \widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)=\inf_v\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}_X(v),\qquad d(x,X)=\frac{\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)}{n^n}.$$
- **Ordinary-double-point density threshold** ([source](#cone:eq-threshold)): $$\label{cone:eq-threshold} p_k=2\left(\frac{k-1}{k}\right)^k.$$
- **Singular-density supremum** ([source](#cone:eq-threshold)): Let $M_k$ be the supremum of $d(x,X)$ over all singular boundary-zero klt $k$-fold germs: the varieties are normal and complex algebraic, and their canonical divisors are $\mathbb Q$-Cartier.
- **Reeb vector** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A Reeb vector is a real torus grading positive on every nonconstant homogeneous function.
- **Quotient orbifold** ([source](#stmt-cone-orbifold)): For a primitive positive integral grading $\zeta$, of canonical weight $r$, let $$B=[(C\setminus\{o\})/\mathbb G_m].$$
- **Leading Taylor semigroup, congruence lattice, and closed cone** ([source](#jet:semigroup)): Write $\operatorname{in}(s)=z^\gamma$ for the leading monomial, omitting its nonzero scalar coefficient, and put $$\begin{aligned} \Gamma&=\{(j,\gamma):0\ne s\in S_j, \ \operatorname{in}(s)=z^\gamma\}, \label{jet:semigroup}\\ \Lambda&=\{(j,\gamma)\in\mathbb Z\times\mathbb Z^N: j\equiv\textstyle\sum_i b_i\gamma_i\pmod m\}, \label{jet:lattice}\\ K&=\overline{\operatorname{cone}(\Gamma)} \subset\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^N. \label{jet:cone} \end{aligned}$$
- **Scaled degree and lattice-density parameter** ([source](#pm-h-18)): For integrations only, introduce the scaled degree and the number $$\label{jet:scaling} s=\frac r{Nm}\le1,\qquad D_0=\frac{j}{sm}=\frac{Nj}{r}.$$
- **Canonical jet lattice point** ([source](#jet:canonical-vector)): 4. *The vector $$\label{jet:canonical-vector} c_0=(r,\mathbf1)\qquad\text{in the original }(j,\gamma) \text{ coordinates}$$ belongs to $\Lambda$.
- **Moment correction and zeroth moment** ([source](#pm-h-19)): For the current grading put $$\label{jet:moment-notation} B_0(x)=f(x)-\|x\|,\qquad H=\mathbb E e^{-B_0(X)}=\int_{\mathbb R_{\ge0}^N}e^{-f(x)}\,dx.$$
- **Truncated exponential function** ([source](#pm-h-20)): Define the continuously differentiable function $$\label{prob:g} g(y)=\begin{cases} 1,&y\le0,\\ (1+y)e^{-y},&y>0, \end{cases}$$
- **Exponential derivative kernel** ([source](#pm-h-20)): For the proof, put $h=1-g$; then $$\label{prob:kernel} h'(y)=ye^{-y}\mathbf1_{\{y>0\}},\qquad k(y):=h''(y)=(1-y)e^{-y}\mathbf1_{\{y>0\}}$$ holds almost everywhere.
- **Taylor associated graded ring** ([source](#pm-h-35)): Extend the filtration by direct sums in original degree, and write $$\overline S=\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_T S.$$
- **Extended Rees algebra** ([source](#pm-h-36)): If $F$ is a nonnegative decreasing multiplicative integral filtration on $S$, set $F_pS=S$ for $p\leq0$ and use the extended Rees convention $$\label{filt:rees-convention} R_F=\sum_{p\in\mathbb Z}\lambda^{-p}F_pS \subset S[\lambda,\lambda^{-1}].$$
- **Intrinsic description of the second filtration** ([source](#filt:W-intrinsic)): For the distinguished section $v$ fixed above, one has $$\label{filt:W-intrinsic} W_pS=\sum_{a=0}^{p}v^aT_{p-a}S\qquad(p\ge0).$$

| q-density-bootstrap-average | d(o,C) | ≤ | $\frac{p_n}{2}+\frac{M_n}{2}$ | Only the definition of the singular-density supremum is used for the slice. |
| q-inductive-density-supremum | M_n | ≤ | $p_n$ | The inductive setting with lower-dimensional local gap theorems available. |
| q-equality-restriction-volume | \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w) | = | $2^{-N}$ | The normalization specified by the equality-data lemma. |
| q-equality-distinguished-section-value | w(v) | ≤ | $2$ | The equality valuation normalization. |
| q-rounded-maximal-stabilizer | Nm_{\max} | ≤ | $r$ | Sufficiently far terms and the exact rational case. |
| q-weighted-hypersurface-volume-test | \widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X) | ≤ | $\frac{d_F\bigl(\sum_{i=0}^n w_i-d_F\bigr)^n} {\prod_{i=0}^n w_i}$ | The weighted order of the local equation and the chosen positive rational weights. |

In particular $T(v)=0$, and $$\\label{filt:laurent-product}\n \\overline S[\\overline v^{-1}]\n \\simeq \\mathbb C[\\overline v,\\overline v^{-1},u_1,\\ldots,u_N],\n \\qquad T(u_i)=1,\\quad T(\\overline v)=0.$$ The ring $D=\\overline S/(\\overline v)$ is an equidimensional Cohen–Macaulay Gorenstein ring of dimension $N$, with $$\\label{filt:D-series}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hilb}}_T D(z)=(1-z)^{-N},\n \\qquad \\deg\\omega_D=(r-m,N).$$ Its $T$-degree-zero piece is $\\mathbb C$. A bihomogeneous vector-space basis of $D$ can be lifted to sections $e_i\\in S$ such that, on putting $T_i=T(e_i)$, $$\\label{filt:S-basis}\n \\{v^a e_i:a\\in\\mathbb N\\}$$ is a vector-space basis of $S$, compatible with original degree and $T$-order. The Taylor semigroup and its lattice satisfy $$\\label{filt:semigroup-input}\n \\Gamma=K\\cap\\Lambda,\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{int}}(K)\\cap\\Lambda=c_0+\\Gamma,\\qquad\n c_0=(r,1,\\ldots,1).$$ Here $K$ is rational polyhedral. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-smooth-factor-test** [claim](#stmt-cone-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cone-product): Gauss extension, finite-colength decomposition, and a Riemann-sum volume calculation. -> The smooth-factor density inequality.
- **step-lift-cotangent-generators** [claim](#stmt-cone-edim) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cone-edim): Degreewise initial-form cancellation terminates modulo the square of the maximal ideal by the finite-discrepancy Izumi inequality. -> The original cotangent space is spanned by lifted generators.
- **step-compute-odp-minimum** [claim](#stmt-end-odp-value) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-odp-value): Uniqueness of the minimizer, orthogonal irreducibility of homogeneous harmonic pieces, and hypersurface adjunction. -> The ordinary-double-point normalized-volume value, including its analytic form.
- **step-import-fourfold-interface** [claim](#stmt-inp-fourfold) / [proof](#pm-h-10): Use the stated companion theorem as an external base-case interface. -> The fourfold induction base.
- **step-reduce-to-gorenstein-cone** [claim](#stmt-cone-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cone-reduction): Stable degeneration and K-polystable degeneration preserve the minimizing volume; orbit semicontinuity and lower-dimensional slices force a smooth puncture, while the index-cover degree formula forces Gorenstein structure. -> A singular density-preserving Gorenstein K-polystable cone with embedding-dimension control.
- **step-approximate-reeb-ray** [claim](#stmt-cone-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cone-approximation): Lattice recurrence followed by primitive reduction and uniform comparison on fixed generator characters. -> Fast primitive gradings, converging degree-valuation volumes, and generator-controlled stabilizer bounds.
- **step-form-faithful-orbifold** [claim](#stmt-cone-orbifold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cone-orbifold): Take the grading quotient stack, extend homogeneous functions by normality, and contract the canonical form with the Euler field. -> The smooth effective quotient orbifold, faithful polarization, and section-ring identity.
- **step-bound-fixed-lift-deformations** [claim](#stmt-small-deformation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-small-deformation): Apply the fixed-section obstruction theory and orbifold Riemann–Roch, retaining trivial characters in the specified representatives. -> A componentwise deformation-dimension bound and its exceptional case.
- **step-prove-finite-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-small-finite-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-small-finite-evaluation): Proper twisted stable maps and minimal curve degree identify the vertex fiber; graded Nakayama proves module finiteness, and dimension gives surjectivity. -> Finite surjective evaluation supported at the contracted map over the vertex.
- **step-norm-translation-tensor** [claim](#stmt-small-norm-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-small-norm-tensor): Differentiate the additive source translation and take a symmetric product over the generic sheets; integrality and normality extend the tensor regularly. -> A nonzero symmetric tangent tensor of negative integral weight.
- **step-bound-reeb-tensor-weight** [claim](#stmt-small-tensor-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-small-tensor-bound): Bochner subharmonicity and integration of the cone Laplacian over the compact link. -> The lower bound for tensor derivation weight.
- **step-control-tensor-characters** [claim](#stmt-small-character-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-small-character-estimate): Pair with differentials of fixed eigen-generators and bound monomial length to control characters uniformly in tensor rank. -> A rank-uniform character bound and endpoint stabilizer estimate.
- **step-classify-small-stabilizers** [claim](#stmt-small-classification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-small-character-estimate-2): Use integrality to eliminate the approximation error, tangent faithfulness to eliminate stabilizers, and high-index polarized classification to recover the cone. -> A smooth cone or an ordinary double point cone.
- **step-build-jet-semigroup** [claim](#stmt-jet-setup) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-setup): Compatible jet separation gives the generated lattice; a fixed-embedding intersection bound gives coercivity; conductor exhaustion gives nonsaturated asymptotic counting. -> The jet semigroup, closed epigraph cone, lattice density, and canonical lattice point.
- **step-test-degree-and-taylor-order** [claim](#stmt-jet-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-test): Use a local line-bundle extraction, compute discrepancy from the canonical form, and integrate the homogeneous cone count by layers. -> Centered test valuations with explicit discrepancy and volume formulas.
- **step-extract-moment-inequalities** [claim](#stmt-jet-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-moments): Apply the volume hypothesis at zero cost and use homogeneous moments plus uniformly bounded second derivatives to control the right derivative near a minimizing grading. -> The zeroth-moment lower bound and vanishing-error derivative inequality.
- **step-prove-exponential-logconcavity** [claim](#stmt-prob-logconcavity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prob-logconcavity): Begin with a difference of exponential variables, preserve log-concavity under one-sided convolution, and apply Jensen to the distribution function and its complement. -> Full-support log-concavity and barycenter half-space bounds.
- **step-prove-strict-single-crossing** [claim](#stmt-prob-single-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prob-single-crossing): Monotone likelihood-ratio comparisons yield a strict crossing for translated densities and then for exponential scaling, including parameter signs across zero. -> Strict signs on both sides of any kernel-expectation zero.
- **step-classify-finite-expectation-maxima** [claim](#stmt-prob-critical-points) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prob-critical-points): Differentiate under expectation, use conditional integration by parts and exponential size bias, then apply strict crossing and a splitting Hessian. -> The single-exceptional-coefficient shape of every nonzero finite local maximum.
- **step-compute-universal-exponential-bound** [claim](#stmt-prob-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prob-critical-points-2): Control escape to infinity by the centroid bound, pass through increasing dimensions by the law of large numbers, and maximize the resulting scalar expectation with an exponential-series strict-gap certificate. -> The exact universal supremum and its strict gap below the moment threshold.
- **step-force-canonical-interiority** [claim](#stmt-jet-interior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-interior): A hypothetical supporting plane at the canonical exponent gives a sum-zero linear lower bound; the envelope inequality contradicts the moment lower bound. -> The canonical point lies in the interior of the original-degree jet cone.
- **step-establish-orbifold-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-adj-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-adj-vanishing): Retain divisorial inertia in the canonical coarse correction, construct an effective klt coarse boundary, and use exact invariant pushdown and adjoint vanishing. -> Higher cohomology vanishing for the stated divisor.
- **step-lift-canonical-shifted-jets** [claim](#stmt-adj-implication) / [proof](#proof-stmt-adj-implication): Replace finitely many initials by scalar weights, form the weighted blowup stack, check monomial multiplier membership, lift by adjoint vanishing, and push down without poles. -> A global section with the prescribed original Taylor initial.
- **step-prove-polyhedral-saturation** [claim](#stmt-adj-saturation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-adj-saturation): Lattice recurrence excludes irrational or excessive-height supports, canonical interiority bounds the possible integral supports, and adjoint lifting identifies all cone lattice points. -> Rational polyhedrality, primitive canonical facet height, saturation, and interior translation.
- **step-construct-taylor-ring-and-basis** [claim](#stmt-filt-first-ring) / [proof](#proof-stmt-filt-first-ring): Finite terminating subduction and a monomial degeneration transfer normal Gorenstein structure; invert the constant initial and compare free modules over its polynomial parameter. -> The Taylor ring, distinguished section, quotient Hilbert series, and compatible original-ring basis.
- **step-track-rees-discrepancy** [claim](#stmt-filt-discrepancy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-filt-discrepancy): Lift graded generators and the canonical generator, apply Cartier adjunction, compare rational top forms, and take suprema over monomial discrepancy retractions. -> The Rees canonical top form and restriction discrepancy inequality.
- **step-rule-out-nonreduced-quotient** [claim](#stmt-filt-reduced) / [proof](#proof-stmt-filt-reduced): A repeated divisor component gives a monomial Rees valuation with an upper volume bound incompatible with the standing lower volume hypothesis. -> The distinguished-section quotient is reduced.
- **step-identify-second-associated-graded** [claim](#stmt-filt-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-filt-structure): Expand products in the compatible basis and identify the equality-cost terms with the quotient product; reduced central fibers and Serre conditions give a normal Rees total space. -> The second associated graded polynomial extension and its normal Gorenstein Rees space.
- **step-prove-free-grading-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-filt-free-grading) / [proof](#proof-stmt-filt-free-grading): Use active-generator Bézout identities for torsor charts, degree of the genuine ample line bundle, successive Cartier sections, and a regular homogeneous parameter sequence. -> Polynomial-ring rigidity with degree-one generators.
- **step-find-second-stabilizer** [claim](#stmt-filt-stabilizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-filt-stabilizer): Contrapose free-grading rigidity and lift a hypothetical polynomial central ring to contradict singularity of the original cone. -> A nonvertex point with nontrivial second stabilizer.
- **step-construct-uniformizing-slice-tests** [claim](#stmt-boot-slice-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-slice-tests): Take a homogeneous level slice and its finite étale action chart, extend valuations trivially along the group factor, and compare filtration cutoffs with valuation ideals. -> A same-dimensional slice and centered restricted valuation tests.
- **step-prove-slice-klt** [claim](#stmt-boot-slice-klt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-slice-klt): Approach a hypothetical nonpositive-discrepancy divisor by exactly centered monomial valuations and contradict the cone's lower volume bound. -> Klt at the slice point.
- **step-prove-slice-singular** [claim](#stmt-boot-slice-singular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-slice-singular): A hypothetical smooth slice yields an equivariant formal fixed section in the other deformation, contradicting the fixed locus of its standard affine generic fiber. -> The slice point is singular.
- **step-equidistribute-leading-character-lengths** [claim](#stmt-boot-character-shares) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-character-shares): Multiplication by powers of the nonzero primitive-character parameter injects shifted valuation quotients and squeezes every character length between total-length cutoffs. -> Equal leading character colengths.
- **step-refine-slice-volume-by-one-character** [claim](#stmt-boot-refined-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-refined-estimate): Split colength exactly, divide the high-filtration summand by a parameter power, and inject into a single character quotient. -> The optimized split upper bound for cone valuation volume.
- **step-optimize-holder-bootstrap** [claim](#stmt-boot-holder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-holder): Minimize the split expression, apply Hölder with the stated conjugate exponents, and use the singular-density supremum for the klt singular slice. -> The density bootstrap inequality and its Hölder equality criterion.
- **step-close-upper-bound-supremum** [claim](#stmt-boot-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-upper): Choose densities approaching a hypothetical excessive supremum, eliminate the small-stabilizer alternative, and pass the fixed-cone bootstrap inequalities to the supremum. -> The singular-density supremum is bounded by the ordinary-double-point threshold.
- **step-extract-equality-data** [claim](#stmt-boot-equality-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-equality-data): Equality of the endpoints forces equality throughout the optimization chain; normalize the slice parameter and use minimizer uniqueness. -> Rigid stabilizer, discrepancy, volume, split, and Reeb-proportionality identities.
- **step-force-distinguished-section-value** [claim](#stmt-boot-strict-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-strict-count): Inject a single power of the distinguished section times a lower-cost basis span into the first cutoff kernel to detect a strict leading colength loss. -> The distinguished section's value cannot exceed the equality threshold.
- **step-round-equality-stabilizer** [claim](#stmt-boot-rounding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-rounding): A bounded number of fixed generators bounds the changing section's possible characters; absolute approximation error and integrality force the maximal-stabilizer inequality. -> Every sufficiently far equality grading lies in the small-stabilizer range.
- **step-identify-equality-cone** [claim](#stmt-boot-equality-cone) / [proof](#proof-stmt-boot-equality-cone): Apply the small-stabilizer classification to the eventually rounded sequence and exclude the smooth alternative. -> The equality cone is the standard ordinary double point.
- **step-build-weighted-hypersurface-test** [claim](#stmt-end-hypersurface-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-hypersurface-test): Construct weighted primary monomial ideals, bound their log canonical thresholds by inversion of adjunction, and compute multiplicity by filtration inclusions and the hypersurface Hilbert series. -> The weighted normalized-volume upper test.
- **step-force-nondegenerate-quadratic-part** [claim](#stmt-end-hypersurface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-hypersurface): Use equal weights to rule out higher multiplicity, perturb the weight of a missing quadratic direction to obtain strictness, and apply the holomorphic Morse lemma. -> The hypersurface gap and analytic equality characterization.
- **step-complete-local-induction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-hypersurface-2): Combine the supremum upper bound with equality cone reduction; embedding-dimension control makes the original germ a hypersurface, and the weighted equality test identifies its analytic germ. -> The local ordinary-double-point gap with both directions of equality.
- **step-derive-global-fano-consequences** [claim](#stmt-cor-global) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-hypersurface-3): Apply the local-to-global volume inequality at a singular point and use the complementary smooth Fano theorem for the larger equality classification. -> The strict singular global bound and the stated nonsingular equality cases.

```json
          "exact_quote": "The following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *$\\Gamma$ is an additive semigroup with one-dimensional leaves, and its generated group is exactly $\\Lambda$. Every finite compatible Taylor jet is realized by sections of all sufficiently large degrees in each admissible progression.*\n\n\n\n2.  *$K$ is a full-dimensional closed convex cone, contains the positive constant ray, projects onto the whole exponent orthant, and satisfies $K=\\overline{\\mathop{\\mathrm{int}}K}$. Its bounded-degree sections are bounded. More precisely, uniformly in the chosen point and nearby grading, $$\\label{jet:bezout-bound}\n     |\\gamma|:=\\sum_i\\gamma_i\\le C_C\\frac jr\n                           \\qquad ((j,\\gamma)\\in K).$$ Its scaled image has the form $$\\label{jet:epigraph}\n     \\widetilde K=\\{(D_0,x):x\\in\\mathbb R_{\\ge0}^N,\\ D_0\\ge f(x)\\},$$ where $f$ is finite, positively homogeneous, convex, and $$\\label{jet:f-coercive}\n     f(x)\\ge c_C|x|\\qquad(x\\in\\mathbb R_{\\ge0}^N)$$ for a constant $c_C>0$ independent of the nearby grading and chosen point.*\n\n\n3.  *In the scaled coordinates the lattice density is $s$. If $\\ell$ is a linear functional strictly positive on $\\widetilde K\\setminus\\{0\\}$, then $$\\label{jet:count}\n     \\begin{aligned}\n     &\\#\\{\\gamma'\\in\\widetilde\\Gamma:\\ell(\\gamma')<q\\}\\\\\n     &\\qquad=s\\,\\operatorname{Leb}\\{(D_0,x)\\in\\widetilde K:\\ell(D_0,x)<1\\}\\,q^n+o(q^n),\n     \\end{aligned}$$ with Lebesgue measure in the scaled coordinates. No saturation assumption on $\\Gamma$ is required.*\n\n\n4.  *The vector $$\\label{jet:canonical-vector}\n     c_0=(r,\\mathbf1)\\qquad\\text{in the original }(j,\\gamma)\n     \\text{ coordinates}$$ belongs to $\\Lambda$. Its scaled coordinates are $(N,\\mathbf1)$.",
```

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-singular-volume-gap**: The normalized volume of a singular germ satisfies the ordinary-double-point bound, and attaining that bound characterizes the analytic ordinary double point.
- **invariant-canonical-shift-saturation**: Canonical interiority forces rational polyhedrality, primitive facet supports of canonical height one, exact lattice saturation, and translation of the interior lattice points by the canonical vector.
- **invariant-supremum-bootstrap**: The lower-dimensional local theorem suffices to bound the supremum of singular densities in the current dimension. The argument requires neither attainment of that supremum nor approximation estimates uniform over different cones.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-smooth-point-gap**: Apply the singular normalized-volume bound to a smooth point. -> The singularity hypothesis is missing; smooth points instead attain the smooth maximum.
- **anti-use-boundary-or-non-klt-extension**: Use the local gap theorem for a nonzero-boundary pair or a germ whose klt condition has not been established. -> The stated theorem supplies no bound or equality classification in that enlarged regime.
- **anti-use-coarse-quotient-character-loss**: Replace the orbifold polarization by a coarse line bundle before resolving the stabilizer action. -> The replacement discards the faithful cyclic characters used in the curve, jet, and slice comparisons.
- **anti-use-asymptotic-count-as-saturation**: Infer exact semigroup membership from the leading lattice-count asymptotic alone. -> An asymptotic count does not establish exact saturation or eliminate missing lattice points.
- **anti-use-same-dimensional-circular-bound**: Assume the desired ordinary-double-point bound for the same-dimensional slice when proving the induction step. -> That assumption would make the bootstrap circular.
- **anti-use-identify-distinct-stabilizers**: Identify the original grading stabilizer with the stabilizer created by the second filtration. -> The quadratic-cone example exhibits different stabilizer orders for these actions, so substituting one for the other invalidates the character-share comparison.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves a gap and analytic equality characterization for arbitrary complex algebraic klt germs. Its normalized volume is an infimum over centered valuations, its jet counting is asymptotic, and its induction uses a supremum that need not be attained. The proof explicitly states: “Neither an attained supremum nor approximation estimates uniform over different cones are required.” These results do not supply a finite input representation or an effective decision procedure for checking the geometric hypotheses, computing the valuation infimum, or constructing the analytic classification. Finite arithmetic checks of auxiliary inequalities would certify only those checks, not this theorem or its geometric constructions.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-local-geometric-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The local gap and analytic equality characterization require a singular closed point of a normal complex algebraic variety, a rationally Cartier canonical divisor, klt near the point, and zero boundary. Blocked use: ['Apply the singular gap at a smooth point.', 'Extend the theorem to nonnormal or non-klt germs, nonzero-boundary pairs, or germs without the stated canonical-divisor hypothesis.']
- **limit-complex-ground-field** (formal_scope): The geometric framework is over the complex numbers, and the equality classification is analytic in that setting. Blocked use: ['Transfer the proof or equality characterization to positive or mixed characteristic without a separate theorem.']
- **limit-no-extra-original-singularity-restrictions** (formal_scope): Isolatedness, complete-intersection structure, quotient structure, and smoothability are not additional assumptions on the original germ. Smoothness of the reduced cone's puncture is a conclusion of the reduction. Blocked use: ['Present the main theorem as restricted to isolated or smoothable singularities.', 'Assume the original germ already has the structural properties subsequently proved for its degeneration.']
- **limit-infimum-versus-test-value** (nonconstructive): Normalized volume is an infimum over centered valuations. A chosen valuation supplies an upper test, and a finite arithmetic evaluation does not establish that the test computes the infimum. Blocked use: ['Report the value of an arbitrary valuation as the exact normalized volume.', 'Advertise a numerical evaluation of the gap expression as an algorithm computing normalized volume.']
- **limit-infinite-discrepancy-convention** (edge_case): Infinite discrepancy is assigned infinite normalized volume directly; the definition avoids an undefined product of infinity and zero. Blocked use: ['Compute normalized volume for an infinite-discrepancy valuation by multiplying its discrepancy power by a potentially zero volume.']
- **limit-cone-reduction-inductive-regime** (hypothesis): The structural cone reduction is used under the stated lower-dimensional induction and density threshold. It is not asserted as an unrestricted structural classification of every singular klt germ. Blocked use: ['Deduce a smooth puncture or Gorenstein structure from klt alone.', 'Use the high-density cone reduction while omitting the induction or density assumptions.']
- **limit-fixed-cone-approximation** (approximation): Fast primitive grading estimates are made on a fixed cone. Their constants and sufficiently far regime are not uniform across the class defining the singular-density supremum; a rational minimizing ray permits an exact constant sequence. Blocked use: ['Infer a uniform stopping index across unrelated cones.', 'Replace absolute cocharacter convergence with a weaker approximation when using integral rounding.']
- **limit-small-stabilizer-branch** (hypothesis): The small-stabilizer classification requires primitive positive integral gradings with the specified fast approximation and stabilizer inequality. Its deformation argument also distinguishes the exceptional trivial tangent-action case. Blocked use: ['Classify an arbitrary positively graded cone as smooth or an ordinary double point.', 'Apply finite evaluation without excluding the stated deformation exception.']
- **limit-retain-orbifold-characters** (formal_scope): The quotient stack and faithful weight-one line bundle retain cyclic characters needed by the curve, jet, and slice arguments. A coarse polarization cannot replace them before stabilizers are proved trivial. Blocked use: ['Discard inertia characters when constructing compatible Taylor jets.', 'Apply the ordinary smooth polarized classification before establishing a free grading action.']
- **limit-jet-count-is-asymptotic** (approximation): The jet count controls the leading asymptotic colength in its compatible lattice and scaled coordinates. It does not alone prove exact saturation, and original degree must remain distinct from scaled degree. Blocked use: ['Infer exact Taylor-semigroup membership from leading lattice-count agreement.', 'Use scaled degree in an original-degree congruence or canonical-shift identity.']
- **limit-exponential-expectation-regime** (hypothesis): The universal expectation bound uses independent mean-one exponentials, a coefficient vector with zero sum, the stated truncated exponential function, and the theorem's fixed shift. Its dimension-uniform supremum is approached through increasing dimension. Blocked use: ['Use the constant as a generic tail-probability bound for arbitrary distributions.', 'Assume that the displayed limiting optimizer establishes a finite-dimensional attained maximum.']
- **limit-adjoint-lifting-and-saturation-order** (hypothesis): Adjoint lifting requires a compatible lattice index whose canonical translate is interior. Exact saturation additionally uses canonical interiority; the lifting implication itself does not assume that interiority beforehand. Blocked use: ['Conclude saturation from convexity or asymptotic counting alone.', 'Make the adjoint lifting proof depend circularly on the later saturation conclusion.']
- **limit-orbifold-vanishing-positivity** (hypothesis): Orbifold vanishing requires the stated smooth proper stack, trivial generic stabilizer, projective coarse space, effective simple normal crossing rational boundary with coefficients less than one, and Cartier divisor with nef and big adjoint difference. Blocked use: ['Apply the vanishing assertion on an arbitrary singular stack.', 'Drop the boundary coefficient, Cartier, or adjoint positivity conditions.']
- **limit-canonical-translation-ring-construction** (hypothesis): The Gorenstein canonical shift of the Taylor graded ring uses the interior-translation identity in addition to saturation. The subsequent construction uses a distinguished constant-initial section and a basis compatible with original degree and Taylor order. Blocked use: ['Infer the specified canonical generator from saturation alone.', 'Construct the second filtration from arbitrary incompatible basis lifts.']
- **limit-rees-discrepancy-preconditions** (hypothesis): The Rees discrepancy comparison requires a multiplicative integral filtration respecting original degree, finite length in each homogeneous piece, finite generation, normal Gorenstein total space, the specified central canonical generator, positive parameter value, and a restriction centered at the cone vertex. Blocked use: ['Use the discrepancy correction for an arbitrary filtration or nonnormal total space.', 'Omit the canonical filtration shift or apply the centered normalized-volume test to a restriction with a different center.']
- **limit-reducedness-and-central-normality** (hypothesis): Reducedness of the distinguished-section quotient is proved using the volume lower hypothesis. Normality of the second Rees total space does not require normality of its central ring and does not assert normality of the quotient. Blocked use: ['Infer reducedness from the Hilbert series alone.', 'Infer normality of the quotient or second associated graded ring from normality of the total space.']
- **limit-distinct-stabilizer-actions** (edge_case): The original integral-grading stabilizer and the second-filtration stabilizer belong to different actions. The quadratic-cone example distinguishes them while preserving the original dimension of the slice. Blocked use: ['Substitute the original maximal stabilizer into the slice character-share estimate.', 'Treat the uniformizing slice as a lower-dimensional germ.']
- **limit-character-share-asymptotic** (approximation): Equal character shares are leading asymptotic colength statements for the invariant minimizing slice valuation. The proof uses a nonzero function of primitive character and does not assert equality of finite-cutoff character dimensions. Blocked use: ['Replace every finite character quotient by an exactly equal fraction of total length.', 'Use the character comparison without invariance of the valuation ideals or the primitive-character parameter.']
- **limit-slice-bootstrap-without-circular-assumption** (nonconstructive): The same-dimensional slice must be proved singular and klt before its density can be compared with the defining supremum. The upper-bound proof neither assumes the desired same-dimensional theorem nor requires the supremum to be attained. Blocked use: ['Apply the inductive hypothesis directly to the same-dimensional slice.', 'Assume an attained maximizing germ or uniform grading estimates across the supremum class.']
- **limit-equality-rounding-regime** (hypothesis): The strict distinguished-section count and integral rounding apply to a fixed equality cone with the prescribed valuation normalization and fast primitive gradings. They do not classify a general cone below the equality threshold. Blocked use: ['Use equality identities for a cone whose density does not equal the threshold.', 'Round a nonvanishing approximation error as though it were eventually smaller than an integral gap.']
- **limit-weighted-hypersurface-upper-test** (hypothesis): The weighted expression is an upper test for a klt hypersurface in regular ambient parameters with positive rational weights. The weighted initial equation need not be irreducible or reduced, and equality classification requires the separate quadratic-part argument. Blocked use: ['Treat the chosen weighted upper expression as the exact normalized volume.', 'Apply the hypersurface test to arbitrary equations without certifying klt and regular ambient parameters.', 'Reject the test solely because its weighted initial equation is reducible or nonreduced.']
- **limit-global-fano-hypotheses-and-sharpness** (formal_scope): The global bounds require K-semistability and the stated rational Fano hypotheses. The smaller singular bound has no asserted sharpness or equality classification; the larger bound excludes projective space and has the stated smooth equality cases. Blocked use: ['Extend the global degree bound to arbitrary projective varieties.', 'Claim a classification of equality for the smaller singular bound.', "Include projective space in the larger bound's regime."]

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the ordinary-double-point gap conjecture for boundary-zero complex algebraic klt germs in every dimension: a singular <i>n</i>-dimensional germ has normalized volume at most $`2(n-1)^n`$, with equality precisely at an analytic ordinary double point.
