The two stabilizers and filtrations can already differ for an ordinary double point. Let $$S=\mathbb C[a,z_1,\ldots,z_N,b]/(ab-q(z)),
 \qquad q(z)=\sum_{i=1}^N z_i^2,$$ with all original degrees equal to one. Here $r=N$ and $m=1$. At the quotient point $[a:z:b]=[1:0:0]$, the chart $a=1$ has $b=q(z)$. Taking $v=a$, the three degrees are $$\begin{array}{c@{\qquad}ccc}
 \toprule
 &a&z_i&b\\
 \midrule
 \text{original degree}&1&1&1\\
 T&0&1&2\\
 W&1&1&2\\
 \bottomrule
\end{array}$$ The first Taylor graded ring has the same presentation as $S$, and $D=\mathbb C[z_1,\ldots,z_N,b]/(q(z))$. The family $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\overline S\to\mathbb A^1_t$ defined by $t=\overline v=a$ has invariant parameter for the $T$-action. After inverting $t$, $b=q(z)/a$ gives the polynomial family of Equation <a href="#filt:laurent-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="filt:laurent-product">[filt:laurent-product]</a>. With $\mathsf a=\lambda^{-1}a$, $\mathsf z_i=\lambda^{-1}z_i$, and $\mathsf b=\lambda^{-2}b$, the second Rees space is $$\mathfrak X=
 \{\lambda\mathsf a\mathsf b-q(\mathsf z)=0\}.$$ Its action has weights $(-1,1,1,2)$ on $(\lambda,\mathsf a,\mathsf z_i,\mathsf b)$. The central point with $\mathsf b=1$ and all other coordinates zero therefore has stabilizer $h=2$. Its transverse slice $$Y=\{\mathsf b=1\}
   =\{\lambda\mathsf a-q(\mathsf z)=0\}$$ is again an ordinary double point of dimension $n=N+1$. Thus the original stabilizer $m=1$ and the new stabilizer $h=2$ play different roles, and taking this slice preserves the original dimension.

<a id="pm-h-39"></a>
#### A nontrivial stabilizer on the new central ring

<a id="stmt-filt-free-grading"></a>
<div id="filt:free-grading" class="lemma">

**Lemma 31** (Free gradings and polynomial rings). *Let $E$ be a finitely generated positively graded reduced equidimensional Cohen–Macaulay $\mathbb C$-algebra of dimension $N\geq2$, with $E_0=\mathbb C$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}E(z)=(1-z)^{-N}$. If the grading action has trivial stabilizer at every nonvertex closed point of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}E$, then $E$ is a polynomial ring on $N$ generators of degree one.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-filt-free-grading"><summary>Proof of stmt-filt-free-grading</summary>

<a id="filt:torsor-chart"></a>
*Proof.* We first justify that weight one defines an honest line bundle on $Z=\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}E$. Choose positive homogeneous algebra generators $a_j$, with degrees $w_j$. At a nonvertex closed point $q$, its grading stabilizer is $$\mu_{\gcd\{w_j:a_j(q)\ne0\}}.$$ By hypothesis this gcd is one. Invert the product $f$ of those active generators. Bézout’s identity supplies integers $c_j$ with $\sum_j c_jw_j=1$, so $u=\prod_j a_j^{c_j}$ is an invertible homogeneous element of degree one in $E_f$. Consequently $$\label{filt:torsor-chart}
 E_f=(E_f)_0[u,u^{-1}].$$ These open sets cover the puncture: they contain all its closed points, and a finite-type scheme over $\mathbb C$ is Jacobson. Their degree-zero charts cover $Z$. Equation <a href="#filt:torsor-chart" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="filt:torsor-chart">[filt:torsor-chart]</a> gives a Zariski $\mathbb G_m$-torsor over $Z$, and makes $L_Z=\mathcal O_Z(1)$ invertible, with $\mathcal O_Z(k)=L_Z^{\otimes k}$. A positive power of this line bundle is ample by the construction of $\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}$, so $L_Z$ is ample.

The same charts show that $Z$ is reduced, equidimensional, and Cohen–Macaulay, of dimension $N-1$. The Hilbert leading coefficient gives $L_Z^{N-1}=1$. Each irreducible projective component has a positive integral degree for this actual ample line bundle. There is therefore only one component, and reducedness makes $Z$ integral. The image $V_0$ of $E_1$ in $H^0(Z,L_Z)$ has dimension $N$: restriction is injective, since a nonzero homogeneous element in a reduced positive-dimensional equidimensional ring cannot vanish on its whole puncture.

We construct successive Cartier sections while tracking the images of $V_0$, not assuming surjectivity onto full spaces of sections. Suppose $Z_i$ is a positive-dimensional integral projective Cohen–Macaulay scheme obtained after $i$ cuts, and that the image $V_i$ of $V_0$ on it has dimension $N-i$. Choose a nonzero section $s_i\in V_i$. Its zero scheme $Z_{i+1}$ is an effective Cartier divisor. It is nonempty, because $L_Z|_{Z_i}$ is ample of positive degree, and it is pure and Cohen–Macaulay. Its degree is again one. The component-degree formula forces a single component of generic multiplicity one; the $S_1$ property then makes this divisor reduced, hence integral. The kernel of restriction $V_i\to H^0(Z_{i+1},L_Z|_{Z_{i+1}})$ is exactly $\mathbb Cs_i$: division by $s_i$ identifies that kernel with regular global functions on the proper integral scheme $Z_i$, and those functions are constant. Thus $\dim V_{i+1}=N-i-1$.

After $N-1$ cuts, the resulting scheme is a single reduced point and the remaining image space has dimension one. Choose a final section nonzero at that point. Lifting all these sections to $E_1$ gives $N$ elements whose common zero on $\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}E$ is empty. Their ideal is therefore irrelevant-primary and they form a homogeneous system of parameters. Cohen–Macaulayness makes the sequence regular. The quotient has Hilbert series $$(1-z)^N\mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}E(z)=1.$$ It is $\mathbb C$, so induction on degree, or graded Nakayama, shows that these $N$ linear elements generate $E$. The resulting surjection from a polynomial ring on $N$ degree-one variables is an isomorphism by the same Hilbert series. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-filt-stabilizer"></a>
<div id="filt:stabilizer" class="corollary">

**Corollary 32** (A nontrivial second stabilizer). *There is a nonvertex closed point of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}D$ whose $T$-grading stabilizer has order $h\geq2$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-filt-stabilizer"><summary>Proof of stmt-filt-stabilizer</summary>

*Proof.* Otherwise Lemma <a href="#filt:free-grading" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="filt:free-grading">31</a> would make $D$ a polynomial ring on $N$ degree-one generators, and Proposition <a href="#filt:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="filt:structure">30</a> would make $G$ a polynomial ring on $n=N+1$ generators. Choose these generators homogeneous also for original degree and lift them to $S$. Terminating cancellation in the $W$-filtration shows that the lifts generate $S$. A surjection from a polynomial ring on $n$ variables to the $n$-dimensional domain $S$ has zero kernel. Thus $C$ would be smooth, contrary to our standing hypothesis. Stabilizers at nonvertex points of a positive grading are finite, so the asserted order $h\geq2$ exists. ◻

</details>

<a id="boot:section"></a>
### The slice estimate and the supremum bootstrap

Throughout this section, $n=N+1\geq 5$, and the local theorem is assumed in dimensions smaller than $n$. Recall that $p_n=2(N/n)^n$ and that $M_n$ is the supremum of $d(x,X)$ over all singular boundary-zero klt germs of dimension $n$. The smooth-value upper bound gives $M_n\leq 1$; no bound by $p_n$ is assumed. We first work with a fixed singular cone $o\in C=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}S$ obtained from Proposition <a href="#cone:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cone:reduction">7</a>, such that $d(o,C)\geq p_n$. Its minimizing Reeb vector $\xi$ is normalized by $A_C(\operatorname{wt}_\xi)=n$.

<a id="boot:algebra-data"></a>
<a id="boot:polynomial-family"></a>
Choose a sufficiently fast grading from Lemma <a href="#cone:approximation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cone:approximation">8</a> in the large-stabilizer range, and take $m=m_{\max}\geq r/N$. All constructions in Proposition <a href="#filt:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="filt:structure">30</a> then apply. We shall use, in particular, $$\label{boot:algebra-data}
 \bar S=\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_T S,
 \qquad D=\bar S/(\bar v),
 \qquad G=\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}_W S=D[\mathtt v],
 \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Hilb}}_G(z)=(1-z)^{-n}.$$ Here $W(\mathtt v)=1$, $W|_D=T$, and $D$ has a positive $T$-grading. The Rees total space $\mathfrak X=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\mathop{\mathrm{Rees}}_W S$ is normal and Gorenstein. Its parameter $\lambda$ has action weight $-1$, and the action is trivial on $S$. The central ring $G$ has canonical filtration shift $n$. We also use the family $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\bar S\to\mathbb A^1_t$ defined by $t=\bar v$, whose parameter has $T$-degree zero. Away from $t=0$ it has the graded description $$\label{boot:polynomial-family}
 \bar S[\bar v^{-1}]
 \simeq \mathbb C[\bar v,\bar v^{-1}][u_1,\ldots,u_N],
 \qquad T(\bar v)=0,\quad T(u_i)=1.$$ The equivariance in Equation <a href="#boot:polynomial-family" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:polynomial-family">[boot:polynomial-family]</a> will be essential below.

<a id="pm-h-41"></a>
#### A slice through a point with nontrivial stabilizer

By Corollary <a href="#filt:stabilizer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="filt:stabilizer">32</a>, there is a nonvertex closed point $y_D\in\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}D$ whose $T$-grading stabilizer is $\mu_h$, with $h\geq2$. Set $y=(y_D,0)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}D[\mathtt v]$, considered as a point of the central fiber of $\mathfrak X$. Its stabilizer for the Rees action is the same $\mu_h$.

<a id="stmt-boot-slice-tests"></a>
<div id="boot:slice-tests" class="lemma">

<a id="boot:basic-test"></a>
**Lemma 33** (The uniformizing slice and its tests). *There is a normal Gorenstein algebraic germ $y\in Y$ of dimension $n$, preserved by $\mu_h$, with a nonzero function $\lambda_Y\in\mathfrak m_{Y,y}$ of character $-1$, such that $Y$ is klt away from $\lambda_Y=0$. Every quasi-monomial valuation $u$ centered at $y$ gives a quasi-monomial valuation $w$ centered at $o\in C$. Writing $\ell=u(\lambda_Y)>0$, one has $$\label{boot:basic-test}
 A_C(w)\leq N\ell+A_Y(u),
 \qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)\leq \ell^{-n}.$$ The comparison is valid before klt at $y$ has been established.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boot-slice-tests"><summary>Proof of stmt-boot-slice-tests</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a positive-$W$-degree homogeneous function $f$ on $\mathfrak X$ with $f(y)\neq0$. Such a function exists because $y_D$ is not the vertex of the positively graded ring $D$; lift a nonvanishing homogeneous function from the central fiber. Write $d=W(f)>0$ and $c=f(y)$. On the open set $U=\{f\neq0\}\subset\mathfrak X$, take the level slice $Y_0=\{f=c\}$. The action map $$\theta:Y_0\times\mathbb G_m\longrightarrow U,
 \qquad (q,a)\longmapsto a\cdot q,$$ is finite étale: it is the cover obtained by adjoining a root $a^d=f/c$ of an invertible function. We take the germ of $Y_0$ at $y$ and denote it by $Y$. Normality and the Gorenstein property descend from the étale product description, and $\dim Y=\dim\mathfrak X-1=n$. The subgroup $\mu_h$ fixes $y$ and preserves the level slice, since $h$ divides $d$.

The restriction $\lambda_Y$ of $\lambda$ has character $-1$. It is a nonzero divisor on $Y$: its pullback to $Y\times\mathbb G_m$ differs from the pullback of $\lambda$ only by the invertible group character $a^{-1}$. It vanishes at $y$. Since $\mathfrak X_{\lambda\neq0}\simeq C\times\mathbb G_m$ is klt, the étale product description also shows that $Y$ is klt away from $\lambda_Y=0$.

We record the function-field relation to $C$. In $K(C)(\lambda)$ the action fixes $K(C)$ and gives $\lambda$ weight $-1$, so $$f=\lambda^{-d}s\quad\text{for some }s\in S,
 \qquad
 \lambda_Y^d=s/c\quad\text{on }Y.$$ After inverting $\lambda_Y$, each component of the slice is finite over the open set $\{s\neq0\}\subset C$. It dominates this open set: the equation is monic in $\lambda_Y$, and the resulting finite algebra is flat over $S[s^{-1}]$. Moreover $\lambda_Y$ does not vanish identically on any component through $y$. Thus $K(Y)$ is a finite extension of $K(C)$, and $S$ injects into $K(Y)$.

<a id="boot:etale-discrepancy"></a>
Extend $u$ to $K(Y)(a)$ by the Gauss rule with $a$ of value zero and residue transcendental over the residue field of $u$. This is the extension trivial on the group factor. Denote by $V$ its restriction through $\theta$ to $K(\mathfrak X)$. Its center is the generic point of the orbit through $y$, and $$\label{boot:etale-discrepancy}
 A_{\mathfrak X}(V)=A_Y(u),
 \qquad V(\lambda)=\ell.$$ Indeed, on the product a log smooth model defining $u$ may simply be multiplied by $\mathbb G_m$; the group parameter contributes no discrepancy. Pulling back log smooth models by the finite étale chart preserves the canonical divisor and discrepancy in the restricted-value scaling. These assertions do not require $u$ to be $\mu_h$-invariant.

<a id="boot:restriction-values"></a>
Let $w=V|_{K(C)}$. Since elements of $S$ are invariant under the Rees action, their pullbacks to $Y\times\mathbb G_m$ are independent of $a$, and $$\label{boot:restriction-values}
 w(s')=u(s'|_Y)\qquad(s'\in S).$$ Equivalently, $w$ is the restriction of $u$ along the finite extension $K(Y)/K(C)$. The Abhyankar equality is preserved by finite extension and restriction, so $w$ is quasi-monomial (Jonsson and Mustaţă 2012, Proposition 3.7) <a id="pm-cite-80"></a>[[JM12](#ref-JM12)].

<a id="boot:filtration-domination"></a>
Write $F^pS=\{W\geq p\}$ for the second filtration. If $s'\in F^pS$, the Rees function $\lambda^{-p}s'$ is regular on $\mathfrak X$, hence its restriction is regular at $y$. Therefore $$\label{boot:filtration-domination}
 w(s')\geq p\ell.$$ Since $F^1S$ is the vertex maximal ideal, this proves that $w$ is centered exactly at $o$. Applying Lemma <a href="#filt:discrepancy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="filt:discrepancy">28</a> with canonical shift $Q=n$, and then Equation <a href="#boot:etale-discrepancy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:etale-discrepancy">[boot:etale-discrepancy]</a>, gives the discrepancy inequality in Equation <a href="#boot:basic-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:basic-test">[boot:basic-test]</a>.

<a id="boot:hilbert-cutoff"></a>
Finally, Equation <a href="#boot:algebra-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:algebra-data">[boot:algebra-data]</a> implies $$\label{boot:hilbert-cutoff}
 \dim_\mathbb CS/F^pS=\frac{p^n}{n!}+O(p^{n-1}).$$ For $p=\lceil k/\ell\rceil$, Equation <a href="#boot:filtration-domination" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:filtration-domination">[boot:filtration-domination]</a> gives $F^pS\subseteq\mathfrak a_k(w)$. The valuation ideal has support only at $o$, so its colength is the same before and after localizing $S$ at the vertex. Taking leading coefficients in Equation <a href="#boot:hilbert-cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:hilbert-cutoff">[boot:hilbert-cutoff]</a> proves $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)\leq\ell^{-n}$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-boot-slice-klt"></a>
<div id="boot:slice-klt" class="lemma">

**Lemma 34** (The slice is klt). *The germ $y\in Y$ is klt.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boot-slice-klt"><summary>Proof of stmt-boot-slice-klt</summary>

*Proof.* If it were not klt, there would be a prime divisor $E$ on a log resolution of $Y$ with $A_Y(E)\leq0$ and center containing $y$. By Lemma <a href="#boot:slice-tests" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:slice-tests">33</a>, its center is contained in $\lambda_Y=0$, so $a=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(\lambda_Y)>0$. Choose a log resolution also resolving this function and the ideal of $y$, and choose a closed point $q$ of $E$ over $y$. In local regular parameters at $q$, give a parameter defining $E$ cost $1$ and the other parameters positive costs tending to zero. The resulting quasi-monomial valuations $u_\varepsilon$, centered exactly at $y$, satisfy $$A_Y(u_\varepsilon)=A_Y(E)+O(\varepsilon),
 \qquad
 \ell_\varepsilon=u_\varepsilon(\lambda_Y)
                  =a+O(\varepsilon).$$ Their restrictions $w_\varepsilon$ are centered at $o$. Since $C$ is klt, $A_C(w_\varepsilon)>0$; hence Equation <a href="#boot:basic-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:basic-test">[boot:basic-test]</a> either already gives a contradiction through a nonpositive upper bound for this discrepancy, or gives $$2N^n\leq\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(o,C)
 \leq A_C(w_\varepsilon)^n\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w_\varepsilon)
 \leq
 \left(N+
 \frac{A_Y(u_\varepsilon)}{\ell_\varepsilon}\right)^n.$$ The quantity inside the last power is positive whenever this chain is applicable, and its limit superior is at most $N$. Letting $\varepsilon$ tend to zero contradicts $2N^n>N^n$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-boot-slice-singular"></a>
<div id="boot:slice-singular" class="lemma">

**Lemma 35** (Singularity of the slice). *The germ $y\in Y$ is singular.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boot-slice-singular"><summary>Proof of stmt-boot-slice-singular</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose that $Y$ were smooth at $y$. The étale product chart then implies that $\mathfrak X$ is smooth at the corresponding point. In its regular local ring, $\lambda$ is an eigenfunction of character $-1$ for $\mu_h$. Lift the product parameter $\mathtt v\in G=D[\mathtt v]$ equivariantly to a function $\widetilde v$ of character $1$. Its differential is nonzero: the class of $\mathtt v$ is nonzero in the cotangent space of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}G$ at $(y_D,0)$, which is a quotient of the cotangent space of $\mathfrak X$.

Consequently $H=\{\widetilde v=0\}\subset\mathfrak X$ is smooth at $y$, and the germ of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}D$ at $y_D$ is the hypersurface cut out in $H$ by $\lambda|_H$. This is a nonzero equation: $\lambda$ and $\widetilde v$ form a regular sequence because $\mathtt v$ is regular in $G$. If $\lambda|_H$ has a nonzero linear part, $D$ is smooth at $y_D$. Otherwise it is a minimal hypersurface equation whose character is $-1\pmod h$, and this character is nontrivial.

We now use the other deformation $\bar S$ over $t=\bar v$. Its special fiber is $D$, and $t$ is invariant under the $T$-action and therefore under $\mu_h$. Complete its local ring at the point $y_D$ of the special fiber. The completion is flat over $\mathbb C[[t]]$. Choose a minimal system of eigen-coordinates of the completed local ring of $D$ and lift them equivariantly. Complete Nakayama gives a surjection $$P=\mathbb C[[t,x_1,\ldots,x_e]]\longrightarrow
 \widehat{\mathcal O}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\bar S,y_D}$$ with kernel $I$, where $t$ is fixed and every $x_i$ is an eigen-coordinate. Flatness identifies $I/tI$ with the kernel of the special-fiber presentation.

If $D$ is smooth at $y_D$, this kernel is zero and Nakayama gives $I=0$. In the other case it is generated by the minimal hypersurface relation of character $-1$. Lift that generator to $I$ and average it to its character to obtain a relation $\widetilde F$ of character $-1$. Nakayama gives $I=(\widetilde F)$. Because the base parameter is invariant and $-1$ is a nontrivial character, one has $$\widetilde F(t,0,\ldots,0)=0.$$ In both cases, substituting $x_1=\cdots=x_e=0$ defines a $\mu_h$-fixed formal section over $\mathbb C[[t]]$ through $y_D$.

Since $y_D$ is not the vertex, there is a positive-$T$-degree homogeneous element of $D$ nonzero at $y_D$. Lift it homogeneously to $\bar S$. Its value on the formal section is a unit, so remains nonzero after passing to $\mathbb C((t))$. On the other hand, Equation <a href="#boot:polynomial-family" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:polynomial-family">[boot:polynomial-family]</a> identifies this generic fiber, with its $\mu_h$-action, with standard affine $N$-space: each coordinate has character $1$. For $h\geq2$ its fixed-point scheme is exactly the origin, where every positive-$T$-degree function vanishes. This is the required contradiction. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-42"></a>
#### The character contribution to colength

By Lemma <a href="#boot:slice-klt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:slice-klt">34</a>, a minimizing valuation $u_*$ exists at $y\in Y$ and is quasi-monomial (Xu and Zhuang 2025, Theorem 1.2(1)) <a id="pm-cite-81"></a>[[XZStable](#ref-XZStable)]. It is invariant under $\mu_h$: this follows from uniqueness up to scaling and invariance of log discrepancy under automorphisms, or directly from (Xu and Zhuang 2021, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-82"></a>[[XZ21](#ref-XZ21)]. Write $$A'=A_Y(u_*),\qquad
 \ell=u_*(\lambda_Y)>0,\qquad
 \sigma=\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(u_*)>0,$$ and let $w$ be the valuation of $C$ constructed in Lemma <a href="#boot:slice-tests" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:slice-tests">33</a>.

<a id="stmt-boot-character-shares"></a>
<div id="boot:character-shares" class="lemma">

**Lemma 36** (Equal leading character lengths). *Let $R=\mathcal O_{Y,y}$ and let $\mathfrak a_t=\{g\in R:u_*(g)\geq t\}$. For every character $\chi\in\mathbb Z/h\mathbb Z$, $$\dim_\mathbb C(R/\mathfrak a_t)_\chi
 =\frac{\sigma}{h\,n!}t^n+o(t^n)
 \qquad(t\longrightarrow\infty).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boot-character-shares"><summary>Proof of stmt-boot-character-shares</summary>

*Proof.* The valuation ideals are $\mu_h$-invariant, so these character quotients are defined. Multiplication by $\lambda_Y^j$ gives an injection $$(R/\mathfrak a_{t-j\ell})_\chi
 \lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow
 (R/\mathfrak a_t)_{\chi-j}
 \qquad(j\geq0).$$ Indeed, the value of a product is the sum of the values, so the kernel is exactly the indicated valuation cutoff. For any two characters one may choose $0\leq j\leq h-1$ connecting them. With $H_0=(h-1)\ell$, this gives, for each fixed $\chi$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{length}}(R/\mathfrak a_{t-H_0})
 \leq h\dim_\mathbb C(R/\mathfrak a_t)_\chi
 \leq \mathop{\mathrm{length}}(R/\mathfrak a_{t+H_0}).$$ The total length has asymptotic $\sigma t^n/n!+o(t^n)$, proving the assertion. The argument uses the primitive character of the nonzero function $\lambda_Y$, and requires no assumption on the tangent representation. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-boot-refined-estimate"></a>
<div id="boot:refined-estimate" class="proposition">

<a id="boot:refined-volume"></a>
**Proposition 37** (The refined slice estimate). *For the minimizing slice valuation and its restriction, $$\label{boot:refined-volume}
 \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)\leq
 \min_{0\leq x\leq1}
 \left\{\frac{x^n}{\ell^n}
                 +(1-x)^n\frac{\sigma}{h}\right\}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boot-refined-estimate"><summary>Proof of stmt-boot-refined-estimate</summary>

<a id="boot:exact-split"></a>
<a id="boot:quotient-injection"></a>
*Proof.* Set $\mathfrak a_k=\{s\in S:w(s)\geq k\}$. For a positive integer $p$, the colength splits exactly as $$\label{boot:exact-split}
 \dim_\mathbb CS/\mathfrak a_k
 =\dim_\mathbb CS/(F^pS+\mathfrak a_k)
  +\dim_\mathbb CF^pS/(F^pS\cap\mathfrak a_k).$$ The first term is bounded by $\dim S/F^pS$. For the second, restriction and division define a linear map $$F^pS\longrightarrow R,
 \qquad s\longmapsto
            \lambda_Y^{-p}(s|_Y).$$ It is regular because $\lambda^{-p}s$ belongs to the Rees algebra. Its image has character $p\pmod h$, since $s$ is invariant and $\lambda_Y$ has character $-1$. By Equation <a href="#boot:restriction-values" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:restriction-values">[boot:restriction-values]</a>, it induces the injection $$\label{boot:quotient-injection}
 \frac{F^pS}{F^pS\cap\mathfrak a_k}
 \lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow
 \left(R/\mathfrak a_{k-p\ell}\right)_{p\bmod h}.$$ In particular, this step is a comparison of one character quotient, not a comparison with the full length upstairs.

Fix $0<x<1$ and take $p=\lfloor kx/\ell\rfloor$. Apply Equation <a href="#boot:hilbert-cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:hilbert-cutoff">[boot:hilbert-cutoff]</a> to the first term in Equation <a href="#boot:exact-split" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:exact-split">[boot:exact-split]</a>, and Lemma <a href="#boot:character-shares" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:character-shares">36</a> to Equation <a href="#boot:quotient-injection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:quotient-injection">[boot:quotient-injection]</a>. There are only finitely many characters for the fixed slice, so the error is uniform as $p\bmod h$ varies. Multiplication by $n!/k^n$ and passage to the limit give $$\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)\leq \frac{x^n}{\ell^n}
                  +(1-x)^n\frac{\sigma}{h}.$$ Continuity extends this bound to the endpoints. Taking its minimum proves Equation <a href="#boot:refined-volume" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:refined-volume">[boot:refined-volume]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-boot-holder"></a>
<div id="boot:holder" class="lemma">

<a id="boot:optimized"></a>
<a id="boot:bootstrap-inequality"></a>
<a id="boot:holder-equality"></a>
**Lemma 38** (Optimization). *For the fixed cone and large-stabilizer construction, $$\begin{aligned}
 d(o,C)
 &\leq
 \frac{\bigl((N/n)\ell+A'/n\bigr)^n}
 {\bigl(\ell^{n/N}+(h/\sigma)^{1/N}\bigr)^N}
 \label{boot:optimized}\\
 &\leq (N/n)^n+(A'/n)^n\sigma/h
 \leq \frac{p_n}{2}+\frac{M_n}{2}.
 \label{boot:bootstrap-inequality}
\end{aligned}$$ Equality in the middle, Hölder, inequality holds precisely when $$\label{boot:holder-equality}
 (A')^N\ell\,\frac{\sigma}{h}=N^N.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boot-holder"><summary>Proof of stmt-boot-holder</summary>

<a id="boot:optimal-split"></a>
*Proof.* For positive $\ell$ and $\sigma$, direct differentiation gives $$\min_{0\leq x\leq1}
 \left\{\ell^{-n}x^n+\frac{\sigma}{h}(1-x)^n\right\}
 =\bigl(\ell^{n/N}+(h/\sigma)^{1/N}\bigr)^{-N}.$$ The minimum is attained at the unique interior point $$\label{boot:optimal-split}
 x=\frac{\ell^{n/N}}
         {\ell^{n/N}+(h/\sigma)^{1/N}}.$$ Together with the discrepancy bound in Equation <a href="#boot:basic-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:basic-test">[boot:basic-test]</a>, this proves Equation <a href="#boot:optimized" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:optimized">[boot:optimized]</a>.

Apply Hölder with conjugate exponents $n/N$ and $n$ to the vectors $$\bigl(\ell,(h/\sigma)^{1/n}\bigr),
 \qquad
 \bigl(N,A'(\sigma/h)^{1/n}\bigr).$$ Their scalar product is $N\ell+A'$. This gives $$(N\ell+A')^n
 \leq
 \bigl(\ell^{n/N}+(h/\sigma)^{1/N}\bigr)^N
 \bigl(N^n+(A')^n\sigma/h\bigr),$$ with equality exactly as in Equation <a href="#boot:holder-equality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:holder-equality">[boot:holder-equality]</a>. Finally, Lemmas <a href="#boot:slice-klt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:slice-klt">34</a> and <a href="#boot:slice-singular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:slice-singular">35</a> show that $y\in Y$ is a singular boundary-zero klt germ of dimension $n$. Since $u_*$ computes its minimum, $$(A'/n)^n\sigma=d(y,Y)\leq M_n.$$ Using $h\geq2$ proves Equation <a href="#boot:bootstrap-inequality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:bootstrap-inequality">[boot:bootstrap-inequality]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-43"></a>
#### The upper bound without a same-dimensional assumption

<a id="stmt-boot-upper"></a>
<div id="boot:upper" class="theorem">

**Theorem 39** (The upper bound). *Assume the local ODP volume theorem in all dimensions smaller than $n$. Then $M_n\leq p_n$. Equivalently, every singular boundary-zero klt $n$-dimensional germ satisfies $$\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)\leq 2(n-1)^n.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boot-upper"><summary>Proof of stmt-boot-upper</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose $M_n>p_n$. Choose singular klt germs whose densities $d_i>p_n$ tend to $M_n$. Proposition <a href="#cone:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cone:reduction">7</a> gives for each of them a singular Gorenstein K-polystable cone $o_i\in C_i$, smooth off the vertex, with the same density $d_i$.

Fix $i$ and choose the fast gradings of Lemma <a href="#cone:approximation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cone:approximation">8</a> for this cone. If $m_{\max}\leq r/N$ holds along an infinite subsequence, Theorem <a href="#small:classification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="small:classification">10</a> implies that $C_i$ is smooth or the standard ODP cone. The first possibility contradicts its singularity. The second contradicts $d_i>p_n$, since the normalized volume at an ODP is $2(n-1)^n$ by Lemma <a href="#end:odp-value" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="end:odp-value">5</a>. Thus the large-stabilizer condition holds for all sufficiently far terms. Applying Lemma <a href="#boot:holder" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:holder">38</a> to one such term gives $$d_i\leq p_n/2+M_n/2.$$ Letting $i$ tend to infinity yields $M_n\leq p_n/2+M_n/2$, contrary to $M_n>p_n$.

All choices of sufficiently fast gradings in this argument are made separately for each fixed cone $C_i$. Neither an attained supremum nor approximation estimates uniform over different cones are required. The slice bound used only the definition of $M_n$, so the argument assumes no same-dimensional ODP estimate. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-44"></a>
#### Equality, a strict count, and the maximal stabilizer

Suppose now that the fixed cone satisfies $d(o,C)=p_n$. Theorem <a href="#boot:upper" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:upper">39</a> is available. For every sufficiently fast grading in the large-stabilizer range, take $m=m_{\max}$ and make the construction above.

<a id="stmt-boot-equality-data"></a>
<div id="boot:equality-data" class="lemma">

<a id="boot:equality-identities"></a>
**Lemma 40** (Consequences of equality). *One has $h=2$, $d(y,Y)=p_n$, and $A'=N\ell$. After rescaling $u_*$ so that $\ell=1$, the resulting restriction $w$ satisfies $$\label{boot:equality-identities}
 A'=N,
 \qquad \sigma=2,
 \qquad A_C(w)=2N,
 \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)=2^{-N},
 \qquad w=\frac{2N}{n}\operatorname{wt}_\xi.$$ The optimal splitting fraction in Equation <a href="#boot:refined-volume" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:refined-volume">[boot:refined-volume]</a> is $x=1/2$, and all inequalities producing that estimate are equalities at the level of leading coefficients.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boot-equality-data"><summary>Proof of stmt-boot-equality-data</summary>

*Proof.* The left endpoint of Equations <a href="#boot:optimized" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:optimized">[boot:optimized]</a>–<a href="#boot:bootstrap-inequality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:bootstrap-inequality">[boot:bootstrap-inequality]</a> is now $p_n$, and the right endpoint is at most $p_n$. Every inequality is therefore an equality. In particular, $$h=2,
 \qquad (A')^n\sigma=2N^n.$$ Combining this with Equation <a href="#boot:holder-equality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:holder-equality">[boot:holder-equality]</a> gives $$N^N=(A')^N\ell\,\sigma/2
     =N^n\ell/A',$$ so $A'=N\ell$. Rescaling $u_*$ by $\ell^{-1}$ makes $\ell=1$, hence $A'=N$ and $\sigma=2$. Equation <a href="#boot:optimal-split" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:optimal-split">[boot:optimal-split]</a> then gives $x=1/2$ and the refined volume bound becomes $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)\leq2^{-N}$. The discrepancy bound becomes $A_C(w)\leq2N$.

Since $w$ is an admissible centered valuation, $$2N^n=\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(o,C)
 \leq A_C(w)^n\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)
 \leq (2N)^n2^{-N}=2N^n.$$ Both factors are positive. Thus both displayed upper bounds are attained, and $w$ computes the normalized volume minimum. Uniqueness up to scaling (Xu and Zhuang 2021, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-83"></a>[[XZ21](#ref-XZ21)], together with $A_C(\operatorname{wt}_\xi)=n$, gives the last identity in Equation <a href="#boot:equality-identities" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:equality-identities">[boot:equality-identities]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-boot-strict-count"></a>
<div id="boot:strict-count" class="lemma">

**Lemma 41** (The distinguished section cannot have value greater than two). *In the normalization of Lemma <a href="#boot:equality-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:equality-data">40</a>, the section $v\in S_m$ used to define the two filtrations satisfies $w(v)\leq2$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boot-strict-count"><summary>Proof of stmt-boot-strict-count</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose $w(v)>2$. Choose a fixed real number $t$ with $$\frac1{w(v)}<t<\frac12,$$ and set $a_k=\lceil tk\rceil$ and $p_k=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$. For large $k$ one has $p_k-a_k>0$. Use the vector-space basis $v^b e_i$ supplied by Lemma <a href="#filt:first-ring" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="filt:first-ring">27</a>; it is adapted to $W$, with $W(v^b e_i)=b+T_i$. Let $E_k$ be the span of its basis vectors with level less than $p_k-a_k$.

Multiplication by $v^{a_k}$ maps $E_k$ injectively into $S/F^{p_k}S$. To see injectivity modulo this filtration, the initial form of $v$ is the regular polynomial variable $\mathtt v$ in $G=D[\mathtt v]$. Hence multiplication by $v^{a_k}$ raises every nonzero initial level by exactly $a_k$ and kills no initial form. Moreover, every element of $v^{a_k}E_k$ has $w$-value greater than $k$: $w$ is nonnegative on $S$ and $a_kw(v)>k$.

Thus the kernel of $$S/F^{p_k}S\longrightarrow S/(F^{p_k}S+\mathfrak a_k)$$ has dimension at least $$\dim E_k
 =\dim S/F^{p_k-a_k}S
 =\frac{(1/2-t)^n}{n!}k^n+o(k^n).$$ This is a strict positive leading-order loss in the first term of the exact splitting Equation <a href="#boot:exact-split" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:exact-split">[boot:exact-split]</a>. The second term still satisfies the character estimate in Equation <a href="#boot:quotient-injection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="boot:quotient-injection">[boot:quotient-injection]</a>. At $x=1/2$, $\ell=1$, and $\sigma/h=1$, these estimates give $$\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(w)
 \leq 2^{-n}+2^{-n}-(1/2-t)^n
 <2^{-N},$$ contradicting Lemma <a href="#boot:equality-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:equality-data">40</a>. Only the single exponent $a_k$ is used at each cutoff, so there is no overlap between collections of powers to count. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-boot-rounding"></a>
<div id="boot:rounding" class="lemma">

**Lemma 42** (Rounding the maximal stabilizer). *Along the fast gradings of the fixed equality cone, the large-stabilizer construction implies $Nm_{\max}\leq r$ for every sufficiently far term.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boot-rounding"><summary>Proof of stmt-boot-rounding</summary>

*Proof.* Choose fixed torus eigen-generators $g_1,\ldots,g_q$ of $S$, with characters $\chi_1,\ldots,\chi_q$. Write $$\zeta=\frac rn\xi+\varepsilon,
 \qquad \|\varepsilon\|\longrightarrow0.$$ The $\xi$-weights of the generators are positive. Consequently there are fixed constants $c_1,c_2>0$ such that their $\zeta$-degrees lie between $c_1r$ and $c_2r$ for all sufficiently far gradings. Also $m=m_{\max}\leq\max_i\deg_\zeta g_i=O(r)$, by Lemma <a href="#cone:approximation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cone:approximation">8</a>.

The section $v\in S_m$ has a polynomial lift in these generators homogeneous for $\zeta$. Every monomial in such a lift uses a bounded number of generators, because its degree is $m=O(r)$ and each generator degree is at least $c_1r$. Its possible torus characters therefore belong to a fixed finite set, independent of the grading and of the chosen section $v$. If $\chi$ is any character occurring nontrivially in $v$, then $$m=\langle\zeta,\chi\rangle
   =\frac rn\langle\xi,\chi\rangle
       +\langle\varepsilon,\chi\rangle,$$ and hence, uniformly for these characters, $$\langle\xi,\chi\rangle
 =\frac{nm}{r}+O(\|\varepsilon\|/r).$$ Taking the minimum over the nonzero character components of $v$ is precisely $\operatorname{wt}_\xi(v)$; the same estimate therefore holds even when $v$ is not itself a torus eigenfunction. Lemma <a href="#boot:equality-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:equality-data">40</a> gives <a id="boot:changing-section-error"></a>$$\label{boot:changing-section-error}
 w(v)=\frac{2Nm}{r}
          +O(\|\varepsilon\|/r)
       =\frac{2Nm}{r}+o(1/r).$$ By Lemma <a href="#boot:strict-count" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:strict-count">41</a>, $w(v)\leq2$. Thus $Nm-r\leq O(\|\varepsilon\|)=o(1)$. The left side is an integer, so it is nonpositive for all sufficiently far terms. On an exact rational ray the error is zero, and the same conclusion is immediate. All constants here concern the fixed cone $C$ only. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-boot-equality-cone"></a>
<div id="boot:equality-cone" class="theorem">

**Theorem 43** (The equality cone). *If a singular cone obtained in Proposition <a href="#cone:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cone:reduction">7</a> satisfies $d(o,C)=p_n$, then it is the standard ordinary double point cone.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-boot-equality-cone"><summary>Proof of stmt-boot-equality-cone</summary>

*Proof.* Take the fast primitive gradings of Lemma <a href="#cone:approximation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cone:approximation">8</a>. A term either already has $Nm_{\max}\leq r$, or lies in the large-stabilizer range. In the latter range, all constructions above are available for sufficiently far terms, and Lemma <a href="#boot:rounding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:rounding">42</a> again gives $Nm_{\max}\leq r$. The entire sequence therefore eventually satisfies the small-stabilizer hypothesis. Theorem <a href="#small:classification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="small:classification">10</a> identifies $C$ with the smooth affine cone or the standard quadratic cone. Since $o\in C$ is singular, only the ordinary double point remains. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:conclusion"></a>
### Equality at the original germ and global consequences

We complete the return from the cone to the original singularity. The lci case of the gap theorem is already known (Liu 2022, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-84"></a>[[Liu22](#ref-Liu22)]. We include the hypersurface argument needed here, so that the source of strictness in the equality case is explicit.

<a id="pm-h-46"></a>
#### A weighted hypersurface bound

<a id="stmt-end-hypersurface-test"></a>
<div id="end:hypersurface-test" class="lemma">

<a id="end:eq-hypersurface-test"></a>
**Lemma 44** (A hypersurface test). *Let $x\in X$ be a klt hypersurface germ of dimension $n$, with equation $F$ in regular ambient parameters $z_0,\ldots,z_n$. For positive rational weights $w_0,\ldots,w_n$, write $d_F$ for the weighted order of $F$. Then $$\label{end:eq-hypersurface-test}
 \widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)\le
           \frac{d_F\bigl(\sum_{i=0}^n w_i-d_F\bigr)^n}
                {\prod_{i=0}^n w_i}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-end-hypersurface-test"><summary>Proof of stmt-end-hypersurface-test</summary>

*Proof.* Both sides of <a href="#end:eq-hypersurface-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="end:eq-hypersurface-test">[end:eq-hypersurface-test]</a> are unchanged by a common scaling of the weights. We may therefore assume that they are positive integers. Choose a positive common multiple $D_1$ of the $w_i$, and in $\mathcal O_{X,x}$ put $$I=(z_i^{D_1/w_i}:0\le i\le n).$$ This ideal is $\mathfrak m_x$-primary.

<a id="end:eq-threshold-test"></a>
The ambient weighted valuation has log discrepancy $\sum_i w_i$, gives value $d_F$ to $F$, and value $D_1$ to the ambient ideal defining $I$. Hypersurface inversion of adjunction (Ein and Mustaţă 2004, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-85"></a>[[EinMustata04](#ref-EinMustata04)], applied to the smooth ambient space with $F$ of coefficient one, gives $$\label{end:eq-threshold-test}
                   \mathop{\mathrm{lct}}_X(I)\le
                   \frac{\sum_i w_i-d_F}{D_1}.$$ In particular the numerator on the right is positive, because $X$ is klt and $I$ is primary.

<a id="end:eq-multiplicity"></a>
Let $J_t$ be the quotient weighted-order filtration on the hypersurface. The elementary monomial floor inequality gives $$J_{(k+n+1)D_1}\subset I^k\subset J_{kD_1}.$$ Indeed, for a monomial of weight at least $(k+n+1)D_1$, the sum of the integers $\lfloor \alpha_i w_i/D_1\rfloor$ is at least $k$. The associated graded of the quotient filtration is $$\mathbb C[z_0,\ldots,z_n]/(\operatorname{in}_w F),\qquad
 H(t)=\frac{1-t^{d_F}}{\prod_i(1-t^{w_i})}.$$ This formula holds even if $\operatorname{in}_w F$ is reducible or nonreduced: it is a nonzero equation in a polynomial ring. The two filtration inclusions and the leading Hilbert coefficient give $$\label{end:eq-multiplicity}
                       e(I)=\frac{D_1^n d_F}{\prod_i w_i}.$$

Choose a divisor $E$ computing $\mathop{\mathrm{lct}}_X(I)$, and put $q=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(I)>0$. Its center is $x$, since $I$ is primary. The inclusion $I^k\subset\mathfrak a_{kq}(E)$ implies $$q^n\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(E)\le e(I).$$ Testing the normalized-volume infimum with $E$, then using <a href="#end:eq-threshold-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="end:eq-threshold-test">[end:eq-threshold-test]</a> and <a href="#end:eq-multiplicity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="end:eq-multiplicity">[end:eq-multiplicity]</a>, yields $$\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)\le A_X(E)^n\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(E)
            \le \mathop{\mathrm{lct}}_X(I)^n e(I),$$ which is <a href="#end:eq-hypersurface-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="end:eq-hypersurface-test">[end:eq-hypersurface-test]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-end-hypersurface"></a>
<div id="end:hypersurface" class="proposition">

**Proposition 45** (Hypersurface equality). *If $x\in X$ is a singular klt hypersurface germ of dimension $n\ge2$, then $$\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)\le2(n-1)^n.$$ If equality holds, the quadratic part of a local equation is nondegenerate, and $(X,x)$ is analytically an ordinary double point.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-end-hypersurface"><summary>Proof of stmt-end-hypersurface</summary>

*Proof.* Let $q=\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x(F)\ge2$. Weights all equal to one in Lemma <a href="#end:hypersurface-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="end:hypersurface-test">44</a> give $$\widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,X)\le q(n+1-q)^n,$$ and klt implies $q<n+1$. The function $b(t)=t(n+1-t)^n$ satisfies $$b'(t)=(n+1)(1-t)(n+1-t)^{n-1}<0
                         \quad(1<t<n+1).$$ Thus $q>2$ gives a strict bound below $2(n-1)^n$.

Suppose $q=2$ and the quadratic part is degenerate. After a linear change of coordinates, choose a direction absent from the quadratic part. Give it weight $1-\epsilon$, and give every other direction weight one, where $0<\epsilon<1/3$ is rational. Every term of ordinary degree at least three has weight greater than two, so $d_F=2$. The bound becomes $$\frac{2(n-1-\epsilon)^n}{1-\epsilon}.$$ Its logarithmic derivative at $\epsilon=0$ is $-n/(n-1)+1=-1/(n-1)<0$. It is therefore strictly smaller than $2(n-1)^n$ for sufficiently small $\epsilon>0$. Equality forces a nondegenerate quadratic part, and the holomorphic Morse Lemma gives the asserted analytic germ. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-47"></a>
#### Completion of the induction

<details id="proof-stmt-end-hypersurface-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-end-hypersurface</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* The cases $n=2,3,4$ were established in Section <a href="#sec:cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cones">2</a>, the last by Theorem <a href="#inp:fourfold" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inp:fourfold">6</a>. Suppose the theorem is known in lower dimensions and let $n=N+1\ge5$. The supremum argument of Theorem <a href="#boot:upper" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:upper">39</a> gives $M_n\le p_n$, hence the asserted inequality for every singular germ.

Now suppose $d(x,X)=p_n$. Apply Proposition <a href="#cone:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cone:reduction">7</a> to obtain a K-polystable cone $C$ with the same density and $\mathop{\mathrm{edim}}(X,x)\le\mathop{\mathrm{edim}}(C,o)$. The equality argument of Theorem <a href="#boot:equality-cone" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="boot:equality-cone">43</a> makes $C$ an ordinary double point. Therefore $\mathop{\mathrm{edim}}(X,x)\le n+1$.

Since $x$ is singular, its embedding dimension is exactly $n+1$, and it is a hypersurface germ. To see this algebraically, start with an embedding in a smooth ambient variety and use equations with independent linear parts to cut the ambient germ down to dimension $n+1$. The remaining ideal is a height-one prime in a regular local ring, so is principal. Proposition <a href="#end:hypersurface" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="end:hypersurface">45</a> shows that its equation has nondegenerate quadratic part and hence that the analytic germ is ODP.

Conversely, Lemma <a href="#end:odp-value" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="end:odp-value">5</a> gives equality at every analytic ODP. This proves both directions of the equality statement and completes the induction. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-48"></a>
#### The global comparison

<details id="proof-stmt-end-hypersurface-3"><summary>Proof of stmt-end-hypersurface</summary>

*Proof of Corollary <a href="#cor:global" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:global">2</a>.* For every closed point $x$ of a K-semistable $\mathbb Q$-Fano $n$-fold $V$, the local-to-global comparison gives $$(-K_V)^n
                    \le\left(\frac{n+1}{n}\right)^n
                         \widehat{\operatorname{vol}}(x,V).$$ This follows from (Liu 2018, Theorem 21) <a id="pm-cite-86"></a>[[Liu18](#ref-Liu18)]; K-semistability and Ding semistability agree in this setting by (Liu 2018, Theorem 13(2)) <a id="pm-cite-87"></a>[[Liu18](#ref-Liu18)]. If $V$ is singular, apply Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> at a singular closed point: $$(-K_V)^n\le
 \left(\frac{n+1}{n}\right)^n2(n-1)^n
 =2\left(\frac{n^2-1}{n}\right)^n<2n^n.$$

If $V$ is smooth and $V\not\cong\mathbb P^n$, the theorem of Li–Miao gives $(-K_V)^n\le2n^n$; its equality cases are a smooth quadric and $\mathbb P^1\times\mathbb P^{n-1}$ (Li and Miao 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-88"></a>[[LM25](#ref-LM25)]. Both have Kähler–Einstein metrics, given by the homogeneous quadric metric and the suitably scaled Fubini–Study product metric, respectively. They are therefore K-polystable (Berman 2016, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-89"></a>[[Berman16](#ref-Berman16)]. Their degrees are $$(-K_{Q^n})^n=(nH)^n=2n^n,\qquad
 (-K_{\mathbb P^1\times\mathbb P^{n-1}})^n
                =(2H_1+nH_2)^n=2n^n.$$ For $n=2$, a smooth quadric surface is $\mathbb P^1\times\mathbb P^1$. The strict singular bound excludes every other equality case. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The induction step $n = N + 1 ≥ 5$ for a singular germ of density at least $p_{n}$. Here $K$ is the cone of leading Taylor indices and $c_{0}$ its canonical lattice point. The same-dimensional slice enters only through the supremum $M_{n}$ of singular $n$-dimensional densities. Equality in the volume estimate returns the cone to the small stabilizer case.",
      "figure_id": "fig:route",
      "pdf_page": 6,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:route",
          "exact_quote": "The induction step \\(n=N+1\\ge5\\) for a singular germ of\ndensity at least \\(p_n\\). Here \\(K\\) is the cone of leading Taylor\nindices and \\(c_0\\) its canonical lattice point.\nThe same-dimensional slice enters only through the supremum \\(M_n\\)\n",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n  proofbox/.style={draw=black!65,rounded corners=2pt,align=center,\n    inner sep=6pt,font=\\small,text width=5.4cm},\n  proofarrow/.style={-{Stealth[length=2mm]},draw=black!75},\n  node distance=8mm and 8mm]\n\\node[proofbox,text width=11.6cm] (cone)\n  {\\(n\\ge5\\), \\(d(x,X)\\ge p_n\\): stable degeneration and induction\\\\\n   An isolated Gorenstein K-polystable cone \\(C\\)};\n\\node[proofbox,below=9mm of cone.south,xshift=-31mm] (small)\n  {\\(m_{\\max}\\le r/N\\)\\\\\n   Minimal curve: direct rigidity\\\\\n   or finite evaluation and tensor estimate};\n\\node[proofbox,below=9mm of cone.south,xshift=31mm] (large)\n  {\\(m_{\\max}\\ge r/N\\)\\\\\n   Canonical point \\(c_0\\in\\Int K\\)\\\\\n   Adjoint lifting and saturation};\n\\node[proofbox,below=8mm of small] (classify)\n  {Smooth projective quotient\\\\\n   \\((\\PP^N,\\cO(1))\\) or \\((Q^N,\\cO(1))\\)\\\\\n   Cone smooth or ODP};\n\\node[proofbox,below=8mm of large] (slice)\n  {Two filtrations and a singular slice \\(Y\\)\\\\\n   Character share \\(1/h\\), \\(h\\ge2\\)};\n\\node[proofbox,below=8mm of slice] (bound)\n  {\\(d(o,C)\\le (p_n+M_n)/2\\)\\\\\n   Hence \\(M_n\\le p_n\\)};\n\\draw[proofarrow] (cone.south) -- ++(0,-4mm) -| (small.north);\n\\draw[proofarrow] (cone.south) -- ++(0,-4mm) -| (large.north);\n\\draw[proofarrow] (small) -- (classify);\n\\draw[proofarrow] (large) -- (slice);\n\\draw[proofarrow] (slice) -- (bound);\n\\coordinate (returnleft) at ($(small.west)+(-7mm,0)$);\n\\coordinate (returnbase) at ($(bound.south)+(0,-7mm)$);\n\\coordinate (returnturn) at (returnleft |- returnbase);\n\\draw[proofarrow,dashed] (bound.south) -- (returnbase)\n  -- node[midway,below,font=\\scriptsize]\n     {Equality and integral rounding} (returnturn)\n  -- (returnleft) -- (small.west);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Flowchart diagram outlining the induction step:\n- Top rectangular node: \"$n \\ge 5$, $d(x, X) \\ge p_n$: stable degeneration and induction \\n An isolated Gorenstein K-polystable cone $C$\"\n- Two downward solid branches from the top node:\n  - Left branch leads to node: \"$m_{\\max} \\le r/N$ \\n Minimal curve: direct rigidity \\n or finite evaluation \\n and tensor estimate\"\n    - Downward solid arrow leads to node: \"Smooth projective quotient \\n $(\\mathbb{P}^N, \\mathcal{O}(1))$ or $(Q^N, \\mathcal{O}(1))$ \\n Cone smooth or ODP\"\n  - Right branch leads to node: \"$m_{\\max} \\ge r/N$ \\n Canonical point $c_0 \\in \\operatorname{int} K$ \\n Adjoint lifting and saturation\"\n    - Downward solid arrow leads to node: \"Two filtrations and \\n a singular slice $Y$ \\n Character share $1/h$, $h \\ge 2$\"\n    - Downward solid arrow leads to node: \"$d(o, C) \\le (p_n + M_n)/2$ \\n Hence $M_n \\le p_n$\"\n- A dashed line with an arrow originates from the bottom of the \"$d(o, C) \\le (p_n + M_n)/2$\" node, extends downwards, routes horizontally to the left underneath the label \"Equality and integral rounding\", and then routes vertically upwards with an arrowhead pointing into the left-hand node \"$m_{\\max} \\le r/N$ / Minimal curve: direct rigidity or finite evaluation and tensor estimate\"."
    }
  ],
